Human decisions now resolved\n\nThese decisions are authoritative for this execution window.\n\n## 1.1 Credential rotation — DEFER\n\n**Decision:** Do **not** rotate credentials now.\n\nReason:\n\n```text\nno active security threat has been identified\nrotation is useful future maintenance\nit is not urgent enough to interrupt current engineering work\n```\n\nRequired action:\n\n- leave the existing credential-rotation runbook intact;\n- keep credential rotation in Future Work / technical debt / Human Attention;\n- do not rotate or revoke current Bridge/LLM worker credentials in this sprint;\n- do not repeatedly raise it as an urgent blocker unless new evidence of compromise appears.\n\nIf an actual compromise or active threat is discovered, stop and escalate.\n\n---\n\n## 1.2 Branch protection — APPROVED\n\n**Decision:** Protect every repository/branch that AI agents actively modify.\n\nRequired policy for protected `main`/production branches:\n\n```text\npull request required\nrequired CI/status checks must pass\nnormal AI-agent identities may NOT bypass protection\nnormal AI-agent work must NOT push directly to main\nautomated AI PR creation/review/merge is allowed\nhuman approval is NOT required on every PR\nhuman administrators retain emergency/direct-main authority\nforce-push disabled for normal agent identities\nbranch deletion disabled for protected production branches\n```\n\nImportant intent:\n\n> Branch protection exists to prevent accidental direct pushes, not to force a human to click “Approve” for every autonomous change.\n\nAI agents may continue the successful current workflow:\n\n```text\nbranch\n→ implementation\n→ tests\n→ PR\n→ required checks\n→ automated/self review\n→ merge when green\n→ safe deployment\n```\n\nHuman administrators may bypass in emergencies or intentionally commit directly when necessary.\n\n---\n\n## 1.3 GPU telemetry retention — APPROVED\n\nUse the previously proposed **Option A**.\n\n### Raw data\n\n```text\ngpu_samples raw retention: 90 days\n```\n\n### Aggregated data\n\n```text\nhourly summaries retention: 2 years\n```\n\n### Partitioning\n\nPostgreSQL partitioning is **deferred** until database size, ingest rate, or query performance demonstrates a real need.\n\nRecord partitioning as technical debt / future scaling work.\n\n### Pruning authorization\n\nAutomatic pruning of raw `gpu_samples` older than 90 days is authorized **only after**:\n\n```text\nhourly rollup table exists\nbackfill completes\naggregation is verified against raw samples\nbackup/recovery point exists\nretention is configuration-backed\ndry-run pruning result is reviewed programmatically\nrollback procedure exists\n```\n\nNever prune first and aggregate later.\n\n---\n\n## 1.4 ADR-0011 — ACCEPTED\n\n**ADR-0011 is accepted.**\n\nCanonical work semantics:\n\n```text\none ACMS Work Item\n=\none measurable goal with a measurable deliverable\n```\n\nA Work Item may contain:\n\n```text\nmultiple execution sessions\nmultiple /new context rotations\nmultiple tasks\nmultiple worker agents\nmultiple commits\nmultiple PRs when necessary\n```\n\nprovided they all contribute to the same measurable goal.\n\nA `/new` caused by context size, model/session rotation, or interruption does **not** create a new Work Item if the measurable goal remains unchanged.\n\nWhen the measurable goal changes:\n\n```text\ncreate a new Work Item\n```\n\nWork Items may share a higher-level:\n\n```text\nJira issue\nProject\nFeature\nProgram\nhuman business goal\n```\n\n### Work creation authority\n\n**Executive Agent:**\n\n```text\nMAY create additional ACMS Work Items\nMAY decompose an approved higher-level goal\nMAY assign/delegate those Work Items\nMAY create internal tasks/assignments\nMUST stay within approved scope/budget\n```\n\n**Subordinate worker agents:**\n\n```text\nMAY execute assigned work\nMAY create implementation subtasks/checklists inside their Work Item\nMAY produce handoffs/recommend next work\nMAY NOT autonomously create new ACMS Work Items\nMAY NOT silently expand scope\n```\n\nIf a worker identifies new work:\n\n```text\nworker records recommendation in handoff\n→ Executive Agent decides whether to create a new Work Item\n```\n\nThis is intended to prevent recursive/unbounded work creation.\n\n---\n\n## 1.5 Jira / ACMS integration — APPROVED DIRECTION\n\nJira remains the **human-facing planning system**.\n\nACMS is the **AI execution/orchestration system beneath it**.\n\nCanonical relationship:\n\n```text\nHuman\n→ Jira issue / Epic / Task\n→ assigned conceptually to Executive Agent\n→ ACMS creates internal Work Items\n→ Executive Agent delegates to workers\n→ ACMS tracks detailed execution\n→ summaries / links / useful status flow back to Jira\n```\n\n### Jira ticket policy\n\nDo **not** create a Jira ticket for every AI microtask.\n\nDo **not** create “bazillions” of Jira issues corresponding to internal Work Items.\n\nInstead:\n\n```text\n1 Jira item\n→ potentially many ACMS Work Items/tasks\n```\n\nACMS internal decomposition stays internal.\n\n### Jira updates allowed\n\nThe integration may automatically:\n\n```text\nread Jira work assigned to the designated AI/Executive account\nread Jira issue descriptions/comments/context\nattach ACMS/Jira correlation IDs\nadd useful status comments\nadd handoff/result summaries\nadd links to artifacts/PRs\nreport completion/blockers\n```\n\nFor v1, avoid complex bidirectional workflow automation unless necessary.\n\nDo not automatically create child Jira tickets for ACMS work.\n\nDo not build Linear as the primary integration.\n\n### Jira vs Linear\n\nCurrent direction:\n\n```text\nJira = primary human work-management system\nLinear = existing synchronized secondary system / fallback\n```\n\nReason:\n\n- Jira supports software and non-software work;\n- Jira is highly customizable;\n- Linear is primarily software-oriented;\n- required Linear features would add unnecessary cost;\n- Startup Teams already has Jira↔Linear synchronization if Linear becomes useful later.\n\nDo not spend engineering time replacing Jira with Linear.\n\n### Jira account\n\nA dedicated full Jira account already exists for:\n\n```text\nmiamtechnologypvtltd@gmail.com\n```\n\nThe Executive Agent integration may use the authorized Jira account/service identity to:\n\n```text\nsee work assigned by humans\nread issue context\npost comments/status summaries\nlink ACMS execution results\n```\n\nNever place Jira passwords, API tokens, recovery codes, or session cookies in Git.\n\nPrefer API token/OAuth/service authentication over interactive browser login where supported.\n\n---\n\n## 1.6 `agentifyme-external-agent-base` — CREATION APPROVED\n\nCreate a **private** repository:\n\n```text\nstartupteams/agentifyme-external-agent-base\n```\n\nPurpose:\n\n> Temporary safe baseline for external/customer-facing AgentifyMe workers, including Speed-to-Lead workers.\n\nThis is explicitly a **temporary architecture**, not necessarily the final long-term Agent State solution.\n\n### Allowed reuse from `hermes-base-setup`\n\nSanitized generic mechanisms may be copied/adapted:\n\n```text\n.gitignore strategy\nsecret scanning\nsafe bootstrap mechanics\nversion/build metadata\nstate-sync patterns\ngeneric deployment helpers\ngeneric documentation structure\n```\n\n### Explicitly forbidden content\n\nDo **not** copy:\n\n```text\nStartup Teams internal profiles\nSOUL/persona files containing internal company context\ninternal memories\ninternal skills\ncompany-only instructions\ninternal customer information\nAPI keys/tokens/secrets\nemployee-specific context\nprivate operational notes\n```\n\n### One-time contamination validation\n\nDuring repository creation:\n\n1. create the sanitized baseline in a temporary worktree/directory;\n2. scan it for known internal profile/memory/skill markers;\n3. inspect Git history/content before first production use;\n4. fail the setup if internal Startup Teams content is found;\n5. correct the source tree before use.\n\nA permanent continuous “internal marker” test is **not required** at this time.\n\nNormal secret scanning remains required.\n\n### Speed-to-Lead\n\nSpeed-to-Lead worker agents may temporarily use this repository as their default agent baseline.\n\nDo not copy the internal `hermes-base-setup` agent branches into it.\n\n---\n\n# 2. Baseline from latest handoff\n\nDo not redo completed work.\n\nExpected production baseline:\n\n```text\nACMS CT122:\n  6f772aa\n  v0.11.0\n  alembic 0008\n\nLLM Manager / ARM VM114:\n  cb82bcd\n  ORM slices 1–4 live\n  Server Manager API healthy\n\nVM124 / acms-worker-001:\n  healthy\n  Bridge proven\n  real budget-gated dispatch proven\n  state sync VERIFIED\n\nAttention:\n  API + UI + SSE live\n\nRecovery:\n  ARM max 5 attempts\n  ACMS max 3 Executive reassignments\n```\n\nVerify current `main`, production SHA, health, and migrations before modifying anything.\n\nIf production moved forward compatibly, use current main rather than resetting to these SHAs.\n\n---\n\n# 3. Phase A — enforce AI-safe branch protection\n\nThis is now explicitly authorized.\n\n## A1. Inventory repositories AI agents modify\n\nAt minimum inspect:\n\n```text\nstartupteams/acms-project-framework\nstartupteams/llm-manager-project-framework\nstartupteams/pdu-marion-ia-usa-project-framework\nstartupteams/hermes-base-setup\nstartupteams/agentifyme-speed-to-lead\n```\n\nAlso include any other repository currently modified by autonomous AI agents.\n\nDo not blindly modify unrelated archival repositories.\n\n## A2. Protection policy\n\nFor each active production/default branch:\n\n```text\nrequire pull request before merge\nrequire existing CI/status checks\nAI/machine identity cannot push directly to protected branch\nAI/machine identity cannot bypass required checks\nno normal force push\nno normal branch deletion\n```\n\nDo **not** require a human approving review merely to merge routine AI-generated PRs unless that repository already has such a requirement.\n\nPreserve:\n\n```text\nhuman administrator emergency bypass/direct-main capability\n```\n\nDocument who/what is considered:\n\n```text\nhuman admin\nAI agent identity\ndeployment identity\nCI identity\n```\n\n## A3. Verification\n\nPerform a harmless verification:\n\n```text\nAI identity direct push → rejected\nPR with failing required check → cannot merge\nPR with all required checks → automated merge path works\nhuman admin emergency path remains documented/available\n```\n\nDo not intentionally break production to test this.\n\n## A4. Documentation\n\nCreate/update:\n\n```text\ndocs/operations/BRANCH_PROTECTION_AND_AI_MERGE_POLICY.md\n```\n\nCheckpoint.\n\n---\n\n# 4. Phase B — execute approved GPU retention architecture\n\nImplement Option A safely.\n\n## B1. Re-measure before migration\n\nRecord:\n\n```text\nrow count\ntable size\ndatabase size\noldest/newest sample\napprox rows/day\napprox MB/day\nimportant query patterns\n```\n\nDo not assume the last handoff's values are still exact.\n\n## B2. Create hourly rollup\n\nAdd an authoritative aggregate table, such as:\n\n```text\ngpu_samples_hourly\n```\n\nUse the actual existing schema and metrics rather than inventing columns.\n\nAggregation must preserve the operational statistics required by LLM Manager.\n\nTypical concepts may include where supported:\n\n```text\nhost/GPU identity\nhour bucket\nsample_count\nmin/max/avg utilization\nmemory metrics\ntemperature\npower\nother currently queried telemetry\n```\n\n## B3. Backfill\n\nBackfill all existing raw history.\n\nUse a resumable/idempotent strategy if practical.\n\n## B4. Verification\n\nFor representative windows compare raw calculations against rollup rows.\n\nRequire deterministic tolerance rules for numeric aggregation.\n\nNo pruning until verification passes.\n\n## B5. Retention configuration\n\nConfigure:\n\n```text\nraw retention = 90 days\nhourly rollup retention = 730 days / 2 years\n```\n\nValues should be configuration-backed rather than embedded in a one-off SQL script.\n\n## B6. Backup\n\nConfirm recoverable backup/recovery point before first destructive prune.\n\n## B7. Dry-run prune\n\nCalculate exactly:\n\n```text\ncutoff\ncandidate row count\ncandidate size\noldest row after prune\n```\n\nLog the dry-run result.\n\n## B8. Execute pruning\n\nAfter all gates above pass:\n\n```text\ndelete only raw rows older than configured retention\nretain hourly aggregates\n```\n\nUse bounded transactions/batches if table behavior requires it.\n\n## B9. Scheduled maintenance\n\nAdd a scheduled rollup + retention job with:\n\n```text\nlocking\nidempotency\nmetrics/logs\nfailure alert/Attention path\n```\n\n## B10. Partitioning\n\nDo not partition now.\n\nCreate/retain TDR/Future Work trigger such as:\n\n```text\npartition if:\ntable growth materially accelerates\nretention delete becomes operationally expensive\nquery latency crosses threshold\nmaintenance/vacuum becomes problematic\n```\n\nCheckpoint.\n\n---\n\n# 5. Phase C — accept and enforce ADR-0011 governance\n\n## C1. ADR\n\nChange ADR-0011:\n\n```text\nProposed → Accepted\n```\n\nInclude the human clarification from this plan.\n\n## C2. Canonical hierarchy\n\nDocument:\n\n```text\nhuman business goal / Jira issue / Feature\n    ↓\nExecutive Agent\n    ├─ Work Item A = measurable goal\n    │   ├─ task/session/worker\n    │   ├─ task/session/worker\n    │   └─ measurable deliverable\n    ├─ Work Item B = measurable goal\n    └─ Work Item C = measurable goal\n```\n\nA Work Item may use multiple agents.\n\n## C3. Creation authorization\n\nEnforce at API/service layer if the current roles support it cleanly:\n\n```text\nAdministrator → may create Work Items\nExecutive Agent → may create Work Items within delegated scope\nordinary Worker → may NOT create Work Items\nObserver → may NOT create Work Items\n```\n\nDo not rely only on UI hiding.\n\nIf “Executive Agent” is not currently a first-class authorization concept, implement the smallest explicit capability/delegation model rather than checking an agent's display name.\n\n## C4. Worker recommendation path\n\nWorkers need a safe way to say:\n\n```text\nI found additional work\n```\n\nwithout creating it.\n\nReuse handoff/Attention/proposal mechanisms where practical.\n\nRecommended structure:\n\n```text\nproposed_next_work:\n  summary\n  reason\n  dependency\n  suggested acceptance criteria\n```\n\nExecutive Agent decides whether to turn it into a Work Item.\n\n## C5. `/new` behavior\n\nDo not create new Work merely because:\n\n```text\ncontext rotated\nsession restarted\nmodel changed\nagent process restarted\n```\n\nif the measurable goal remains the same.\n\nCheckpoint.\n\n---\n\n# 6. Phase D — Jira ↔ Executive Agent v1 integration\n\nImplement the approved **Jira-human / ACMS-execution** model.\n\n## D1. Architecture\n\n```text\nJira issue\n  = human-facing work / goal\n\nExecutive Agent\n  = conceptual AI assignee/orchestrator\n\nACMS Work Items\n  = internal AI decomposition\n\nWorker tasks/sessions\n  = internal execution\n```\n\n## D2. Authentication\n\nUse the designated authorized Jira account/service identity.\n\nNever embed credentials in source.\n\nSupport environment/secret-managed configuration.\n\nIf a Jira API token/site URL/project mapping is not available to the agent:\n\n- implement/test the integration contract with mock/sandbox configuration;\n- create an exact human setup note;\n- do not fabricate credentials.\n\n## D3. Inbound synchronization\n\nImplement a bounded poll/webhook integration that can detect Jira work assigned to the Executive Agent/service identity.\n\nCapture:\n\n```text\nJira issue key\nsummary\ndescription\npriority\nlabels\nstatus\nhuman comments/context\nlinks/attachments metadata where safely supported\n```\n\nCreate/link the corresponding high-level ACMS work context.\n\nDo not create hundreds of ACMS Work Items automatically from arbitrary Jira text without Executive decomposition.\n\n## D4. Executive decomposition\n\nThe Executive Agent may:\n\n```text\nread assigned Jira issue\nunderstand human goal\ncreate one or more ACMS Work Items\ndelegate them internally\ntrack progress\n```\n\nSubordinate workers do not create Jira issues.\n\n## D5. Outbound Jira updates\n\nAutomatically post useful summaries rather than noisy event streams.\n\nExamples:\n\n```text\nexecution started\nmajor milestone completed\nblocked / Human Attention required\nPR/artifact available\ngoal completed\nfinal handoff summary\n```\n\nInclude links/correlation IDs where possible.\n\nDo not post every tool call/session event.\n\n## D6. Jira status mutation\n\nFor v1, keep Jira status changes conservative.\n\nComments/summaries/links are authorized.\n\nIf automatic Jira status transitions are necessary, implement behind an explicit config flag defaulting OFF unless an existing human workflow clearly defines the transitions.\n\n## D7. No Jira microtask explosion\n\nExplicit invariant:\n\n```text\nACMS Work Item creation\n≠\nJira ticket creation\n```\n\nDo not create separate Jira tickets for normal internal AI decomposition.\n\n## D8. Linear\n\nDo not implement a new Linear-native path now.\n\nDocument that existing Jira↔Linear synchronization remains sufficient fallback.\n\nCheckpoint.\n\n---\n\n# 7. Phase E — create private external-agent baseline repository\n\nCreation is now authorized.\n\n## E1. Create repo\n\nCreate:\n\n```text\nstartupteams/agentifyme-external-agent-base\n```\n\nVisibility:\n\n```text\nPRIVATE\n```\n\n## E2. Build from sanitized generic mechanisms\n\nUse `hermes-base-setup` only as a **source of generic patterns**.\n\nDo not fork/copy history blindly if that risks retaining internal content in Git history.\n\nPrefer constructing a clean repository history from reviewed safe files.\n\nAllowed categories:\n\n```text\n.gitignore\nsecret scanner / pre-commit safety\nbootstrap skeleton\nversion metadata\ngeneric state sync\ngeneric README\ngeneric service/timer templates\n```\n\n## E3. {"status": "success", "output": "from fastapi import Header, HTTPException, status\n\nfrom .settings import get_settings\n\n\ndef require_admin_token(authorization: str | None = Header(default=None)) -> None:\n    expected = get_settings().admin_token\n    if expected == \"change-me\":\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,\n            detail=\"ACMS_ADMIN_TOKEN must be configured\",\n        )\n    if not authorization or not authorization.startswith(\"Bearer \"):\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED, detail=\"Missing bearer token\")\n    supplied = authorization.removeprefix(\"Bearer \")\n    if supplied != expected:\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_403_FORBIDDEN, detail=\"Invalid bearer token\")\n=====\n\"\"\"Immutable human-readable work/assignment keys (ACMS-REQ-052; ADR-0010).\n\nAllocation is a durable, transactional counter — never a row count — so keys\nsurvive deletes and concurrent transactions. Backed by the ``acms_key_counters``\ntable (portable across SQLite and PostgreSQL; a PostgreSQL sequence would also\nsatisfy the requirement but the counter table keeps the unit-test path and the\nproduction path identical).\n\nFormat: ``ACMS-WORK-000001`` / ``ACMS-ASG-000001``. Keys are never reused:\nthe counter only ever increments, even when rows are deleted.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom sqlalchemy import String, text\nfrom sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio import AsyncSession\nfrom sqlalchemy.orm import Mapped, mapped_column\n\nfrom .db import Base\n\nWORK_PREFIX = \"ACMS-WORK-\"\nASG_PREFIX = \"ACMS-ASG-\"\n\n\nasync def _next_counter(db: AsyncSession, name: str) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Atomically increment and return the counter for ``name``.\"\"\"\n    await db.execute(\n        text(\n            \"INSERT INTO acms_key_counters (counter_name, counter_value) \"\n            \"VALUES (:name, 0) ON CONFLICT (counter_name) DO NOTHING\"\n        ),\n        {\"name\": name},\n    )\n    row = await db.execute(\n        text(\n            \"UPDATE acms_key_counters SET counter_value = counter_value + 1 \"\n            \"WHERE counter_name = :name RETURNING counter_value\"\n        ),\n        {\"name\": name},\n    )\n    value = row.scalar_one()\n=====\n", "tool_calls_made": 1, "duration_seconds": 0.03}

### [2026-09-29T14:32:04.895375+00:00] assistant

Now I have the full picture. Implementing Phase C — ADR-0012 completion callback. Design:

1. **`acms/completion_callback.py`** — service module: identity binding (task + a2a + agent + runtime), idempotent close, audit events.
2. **`acms/callback_api.py`** — versioned endpoint `POST /api/v1/callbacks/execution-completion` with its own scoped token (`ACMS_CALLBACK_TOKEN`, separate from the admin token — a runtime must not reuse admin authority).
3. **Reconciliation fallback** — extend the scheduler tick to close nonterminal tasks/sessions whose A2A run is terminal on the bridge.
4. **ADR-0012 doc** (Accepted — human decision §1.8).

### [2026-09-29T14:36:42.115152+00:00] assistant

The dedupe ledger used `external_task_id = "disp-<key>"`, but pre-creating the task with `external_task_id=None` (to be back-filled with the A2A run id) breaks that. Fix: keep the dedupe key on the task but in a way that doesn't collide with real A2A run ids. Options:

1. Add a `dedupe_key` column (migration) — heavier.
2. Keep dedupe in the audit event trail — the EXECUTION_DISPATCHED event already carries `dedupe_key` in metadata; query it instead.

Option 2 avoids a migration and uses existing durable state. The `_find_existing_task` must find the task whose dispatch event carries the key. Since the event metadata carries `task_id` + `dedupe_key`, I can query events:

---

### [2026-09-29T14:36:42.143129+00:00] tool · tool: read_file

{"content": "53|async def _find_existing_task(db: AsyncSession, dedupe_key: str) -> Any | None:\n54|    \"\"\"The most recent task created from this dedupe key.\n55|\n56|    Tasks carry the dedupe key as external_task_id ``disp-<key>`` — the\n57|    ExecutionTaskRecord therefore doubles as the idempotency ledger without a\n58|    new table.\n59|    \"\"\"\n60|    rows = (await db.execute(\n61|        select(ExecutionTaskRecord)\n62|        .where(ExecutionTaskRecord.external_task_id == f\"disp-{dedupe_key}\")\n63|        .order_by(ExecutionTaskRecord.started_at.desc())\n64|        .limit(1)\n65|    )).scalars().all()\n66|    return rows[0] if rows else None\n67|\n68|\n69|async def dispatch_work(db: AsyncSession, *, work_item_id: str,\n70|                        instruction: str, agent_id: str | None = None,\n71|", "total_lines": 233, "file_size": 11573, "truncated": true, "hint": "Use offset=71 to continue reading (showing 53-70 of 233 lines)", "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

{"content": "1|\"\"\"Provisioning service — the ARM state machine (§8/§9/§15/§16).\n2|\n3|Sequenced steps, each recorded in provisioning_steps; idempotent on\n4|request_id; writes durable ownership metadata on the VM at create time;\n5|bootstraps Hermes + Agent Bridge; records hermes_state branch metadata.\n6|\"\"\"\n7|from __future__ import annotations\n8|\n9|import json\n10|import logging\n11|import secrets as pysecrets\n12|import uuid\n13|from dataclasses import dataclass\n14|from datetime import datetime, timezone\n15|\n16|from sqlalchemy import select\n17|from sqlalchemy.orm import Session as SASession\n18|\n19|from server_manager.agent_runtime_manager.models.orm import (\n20|    ActualState,\n21|    AgentRuntime,\n22|    BridgeRegistration,\n23|    JobState,\n24|    ProvisioningJob,\n25|    ProvisioningStep,\n26|    RuntimeEvent,\n27|    RuntimeState,\n28|)\n29|from server_manager.agent_runtime_manager.providers.proxmox_vm import (\n30|    ProxmoxVMProvider,\n31|    VMSpec,\n32|    ownership_marker,\n33|)\n34|from server_manager.common.config.settings import get_settings\n35|\n36|log = logging.getLogger(\"arm.provisioning\")\n37|\n38|\n39|@dataclass\n40|class ProvisionRequest:\n41|    acms_agent_id: str\n42|    request_id: str\n43|    name: str\n44|    harness: str = \"hermes\"\n45|    runtime_class: str = \"software_development_worker\"\n46|    site: str = \"marion-ia-usa\"\n47|    model_route: str | None = None\n48|    bridge_profile: dict | None = None\n49|    desired_state: RuntimeState = RuntimeState.DESIRED_RUNNING\n50|    # template selection (defaults to the proven Hermes golden template)\n51|    template_vmid: int = 121\n52|    template_node: str = \"miam00111\"\n53|\n54|\n55|class ProvisioningService:\n56|    def __init__(self, provider: ProxmoxVMProvider | None = None):\n57|        self.provider = provider or ProxmoxVMProvider()\n58|\n59|    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ api\n60|    def submit(self, session: SASession, req: ProvisionRequest, requested_by: str, authority: str) -> tuple[ProvisioningJob, bool]:\n61|        \"\"\"Idempotent submit: same request_id → the original job (§10/§13).\"\"\"\n62|        existing = session.execute(\n63|            select(ProvisioningJob).where(ProvisioningJob.request_id == req.request_id)\n64|        ).scalar_one_or_none()\n65|        if existing is not None:\n66|            return existing, False\n67|\n68|        runtime = AgentRuntime(\n69|            acms_agent_id=req.acms_agent_id,\n70|            provisioning_request_id=req.request_id,\n71|            name=req.name,\n72|            harness=req.harness,\n73|            runtime_class=req.runtime_class,\n74|            site=req.site,\n75|            model_route=req.model_route,\n76|            desired_state=req.desired_state,\n77|            actual_state=ActualState.ABSENT,\n78|            created_by_agent_runtime_manager=True,  # ARM-created VMs carry ownership markers (§8)\n79|            template_source=f\"{req.template_node}/qemu/{req.template_vmid}\",\n80|            ownership_meta={\"bridge_profile\": req.bridge_profile or {}},\n81|        )\n82|        session.add(runtime)\n83|        session.flush()  # assign runtime.runtime_id (Python-side default) before FK use\n84|        job = ProvisioningJob(\n85|            request_id=req.request_id,\n86|            runtime_id=runtime.runtime_id,\n87|            requested_by=requested_by,\n88|            authority=authority,\n89|            state=JobState.QUEUED,\n90|        )\n91|        session.add(job)\n92|        session.add(RuntimeEvent(actor=requested_by, action=\"provision_submitted\", runtime_id=runtime.runtime_id,\n93|                                 detail={\"request_id\": req.request_id, \"authority\": authority}))\n94|        session.commit()\n95|        return job, True\n96|\n97|    def run_job(self, session: SASession, job_id: uuid.UUID) -> None:\n98|        \"\"\"Execute a queued job's steps synchronously (worker loop calls this).\"\"\"\n99|        job = session.get(ProvisioningJob, job_id)\n100|        if job is None or job.state not in (JobState.QUEUED,):\n101|            return\n102|        job.state = JobState.RUNNING\n103|        runtime = session.get(AgentRuntime, job.runtime_id)\n104|        session.commit()\n105|        try:\n106|            vmid, node = self._provision_vm(session, job, runtime)\n107|            self._bootstrap_hermes(session, job, runtime, node, vmid)\n108|            runtime.actual_state = ActualState.RUNNING\n109|            runtime.vmid = vmid\n110|            runtime.node = node\n111|            job.state = JobState.DONE\n112|            session.add(RuntimeEvent(actor=\"arm\", action=\"provision_complete\", runtime_id=runtime.runtime_id,\n113|                                     detail={\"vmid\": vmid, \"node\": node}))\n114|            session.commit()\n115|        except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001 — job failure is a recorded state, not a crash\n116|            log.exception(\"provisioning job %s failed\", job_id)\n117|            job.state = JobState.FAILED\n118|            job.error = f\"{e.__class__.__name__}: {e}\"[:800]\n119|            runtime.actual_state = ActualState.ERROR\n120|            session.add(RuntimeEvent(actor=\"arm\", action=\"provision_failed\", runtime_id=runtime.runtime_id,\n121|                                     result=\"error\", detail={\"error\": job.error}))\n122|            session.commit()\n123|\n124|    # -------------------------------------------------------------- steps\n125|    def _step(self, session: SASession, job: ProvisioningJob, seq: int, step: str, state: str = \"RUNNING\",\n126|              detail: str | None = None) -> None:\n127|        # A step is recorded twice per sequence number (start + terminal update).\n128|        # Upsert on (job_id, seq) so the terminal update replaces the RUNNING row\n129|        # instead of violating uq_provstep_job_seq.\n130|        existing = session.execute(\n131|            select(ProvisioningStep).where(\n132|                ProvisioningStep.job_id == job.job_id, ProvisioningStep.seq == seq\n133|            )\n134|        ).scalar_one_or_none()\n135|        if existing is not None:\n136|            existing.step = step\n137|            existing.state = state\n138|            existing.detail = detail\n139|        else:\n140|            session.add(ProvisioningStep(job_id=job.job_id, seq=seq, step=step,\n141|                                         state=state, detail=detail))\n142|        session.commit()\n143|\n144|\n145|    def _provision_vm(self, session: SASession, job: ProvisioningJob, runtime: AgentRuntime) -> tuple[int, str]:\n146|        s = get_settings()\n147|        self._step(session, job, 1, \"VALIDATING\")\n148|        if runtime.acms_agent_id in (\"\", None):\n149|            raise ValueError(\"acms_agent_id required (§9)\")\n150|        self._step(session, job, 1, \"VALIDATING\", \"DONE\")\n151|        self._step(session, job, 2, \"RESERVING\", \"DONE\")\n152|        self._step(session, job, 3, \"CLONING_VM\")\n153|        vmid = self.provider.next_vmid()\n154|        node = \"miam00111\"  # template host — the clone targets this node\n155|        spec = VMSpec(\n156|            name=runtime.name,\n157|            node=node,\n158|            template_vmid=121,\n159|            template_node=node,\n160|            description_meta=ownership_marker(runtime, node, vmid, runtime.template_source or \"miam00111/qemu/121\"),\n161|        )\n162|        self.provider.clone_template(spec, vmid)\n163|        self.provider.wait_clone_lock_release(node, vmid)\n164|        session.add(RuntimeEvent(actor=\"arm\", action=\"vm_cloned\", runtime_id=runtime.runtime_id,\n165|                                 detail={\"vmid\": vmid, \"node\": node, \"template\": spec.template_vmid}))\n166|        self._step(session, job, 3, \"CLONING_VM\", \"DONE\")\n167|\n168|        self._step(session, job, 4, \"CONFIGURING_CLOUD_INIT\")\n169|        self.provider.configure_cloud_init(spec, vmid)\n170|        self._step(session, job, 4, \"CONFIGURING_CLOUD_INIT\", \"DONE\")\n171|\n172|        self._step(session, job, 5, \"BOOTING\")\n173|        self.provider.start(node, vmid)\n174|        ip = self.provider.wait_for_ip(node, vmid, max_s=420)\n175|        if not ip:\n176|            raise RuntimeError(\"no guest IP after boot (420s)\")\n177|        runtime.ownership_meta[\"ip\"] = ip\n178|        self._step(session, job, 5, \"BOOTING\", \"DONE\", detail=ip)\n179|        return vmid, node\n180|\n181|    def _bootstrap_hermes(self, session: SASession, job: ProvisioningJob, runtime: AgentRuntime,\n182|                          node: str, vmid: int) -> None:\n183|        \"\"\"§9/§10A: Hermes profile init + Agent Bridge credential + Git branch backup.\n184|\n185|        Bridge bootstrap is executed over SSH from the ARM host using the\n186|        provisioning key (the same delivery path the proven AgentManager\n187|        vertical slice used). Bridge credential lives in ARM DB only.\n188|        \"\"\"\n189|        self._step(session, job, 6, \"WAITING_GUEST\", \"DONE\")\n190|\n191|        # bridge credential (stored hashed/last-4 only in metadata; raw value delivered once)\n192|        bridge_token = pysecrets.token_urlsafe(24)\n193|        runtime.ownership_meta[\"bridge_credential_id\"] = f\"bridge-{runtime.runtime_id.hex[:12]}\"\n194|        runtime.ownership_meta[\"bridge_token_last4\"] = bridge_token[-4:]\n195|\n196|        self._step(session, job, 7, \"INJECTING_BRIDGE_CONFIG\")\n197|        runtime.ownership_meta[\"bridge_bootstrap\"] = {\n198|            \"status\": \"deferred_to_step8\",\n199|            \"injected_at\": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(),\n200|        }\n201|        session.add(BridgeRegistration(\n202|            runtime_id=runtime.runtime_id,\n203|            acms_agent_id=runtime.acms_agent_id,\n204|            bridge_credential_id=runtime.ownership_meta[\"bridge_credential_id\"],\n205|        ))\n206|        self._step(session, job, 7, \"INJECTING_BRIDGE_CONFIG\", \"DONE\")\n207|\n208|        # §10A state backup fields are filled by the hermes_bootstrap module\n209|        self._step(session, job, 8, \"HERMES_STATE_BRANCH\")\n210|        runtime.hermes_state_repo = \"startupteams/hermes-base-setup\"\n211|        runtime.hermes_state_branch = f\"agent/{runtime.acms_agent_id[:8]}\"\n212|        runtime.state_sync_health = \"PENDING\"\n213|        self._step(session, job, 8, \"HERMES_STATE_BRANCH\", \"DONE\", detail=runtime.hermes_state_branch)\n214|", "total_lines": 213, "file_size": 9474, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

### [2026-09-29T14:37:42.865443+00:00] tool · tool: read_file

{"content": "1|\"\"\"VM clone identity/network hygiene (2026-09-29 plan §10 / Phase H).\n2|\n3|Root cause being fixed: the VM108/VM124 IP collision — a shared-template clone\n4|kept the template's static netplan identity (10.0.20.203/24), and ARP favored\n5|the most-recently-booted impostor while every app-level check passed.\n6|\n7|Rule: BEFORE a cloned worker may become READY, prove — fail-closed — that:\n8|    unique VMID, unique MAC, unique hostname, unique/reserved IP,\n9|    no inherited conflicting static netplan,\n10|    guest identity matches ARM metadata.\n11|\n12|\"No current ARP response\" is NOT sufficient proof an IP is free: the check\n13|queries the authoritative DHCP/static inventory (Kea leases + OPNsense statics\n14|via `ip`/arp tables of the gateway) AND live probe of every other VM's\n15|ownership metadata. If uniqueness cannot be established, provisioning FAILS.\n16|\"\"\"\n17|\n18|from __future__ import annotations\n19|\n20|import re\n21|import subprocess\n22|from dataclasses import dataclass, field\n23|\n24|\n25|class HygieneError(RuntimeError):\n26|    \"\"\"Raised when uniqueness/identity cannot be established (fail-closed).\"\"\"\n27|\n28|\n29|@dataclass\n30|class HygieneReport:\n31|    vmid: int\n32|    checks: list[tuple[str, bool, str]] = field(default_factory=list)\n33|\n34|    @property\n35|    def ok(self) -> bool:\n36|        return all(passed for _, passed, _ in self.checks)\n37|\n38|    def require(self, name: str, passed: bool, detail: str) -> None:\n39|        self.checks.append((name, passed, detail))\n40|        if not passed:\n41|            raise HygieneError(f\"clone hygiene FAIL [{name}]: {detail}\")\n42|\n43|\n44|# --------------------------------------------------------------------- helpers\n45|\n46|def _run(cmd: list[str], timeout: int = 15) -> str:\n47|    try:\n48|        out = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=timeout)\n49|        return out.stdout + out.stderr\n50|    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):\n51|        return \"\"\n52|\n53|\n54|def _lease_inventory() -> set[str]:\n55|    \"\"\"Authoritative 'IP is claimed' set: Kea leases + OPNsense static mappings +\n56|    live gateway ARP/neigh table. Absence from these sets does NOT prove free —\n57|    it only means unclaimed; the static-netplan scan below is the other half.\"\"\"\n58|    claimed: set[str] = set()\n59|    # 1. Kea lease file (common paths)\n60|    for path in (\"/var/lib/kea/kea-leases4.csv\", \"/var/lib/kea/kea-dhcp4.leases\"):\n61|        try:\n62|            with open(path) as f:\n63|                for line in f:\n64|                    if \",\" in line and not line.startswith(\"#\"):\n65|                        for tok in line.split(\",\"):\n66|                            if re.fullmatch(r\"\\d+\\.\\d+\\.\\d+\\.\\d+\", tok):\n67|                                claimed.add(tok)\n68|        except OSError:\n69|            continue\n70|    # 2. Gateway ARP/neigh (OPNsense or any router we can reach)\n71|    out = _run([\"ip\", \"neigh\", \"show\"])\n72|    for m in re.finditer(r\"(\\d+\\.\\d+\\.\\d+\\.\\d+).*(REACHABLE|STALE|DELAY|PROBE|PERMANENT)\", out):\n73|        claimed.add(m.group(1))\n74|    return claimed\n75|\n76|\n77|def _guest_netplan_static_ips(node: str, vmid: int) -> list[str]:\n78|    \"\"\"Read the guest's effective static addresses via QEMU guest exec.\"\"\"\n79|    script = (\"cat /etc/netplan/*.yaml 2>/dev/null; \"\n80|              \"ip -4 addr show 2>/dev/null\")\n81|    out = _run([\"qm\", \"guest\", \"exec\", str(vmid), \"--\", \"bash\", \"-c\", script], timeout=30)\n82|    return re.findall(r\"\\b(\\d+\\.\\d+\\.\\d+\\.\\d+)\\b\", out)\n83|\n84|\n85|def _known_worker_ips() -> set[str]:\n86|    \"\"\"IPs already owned by other runtimes (from ARM ownership metadata via the\n87|    caller-provided inventory; here read from the agent_runtimes table dump the\n88|    service passes in). Implemented as a callback to avoid DB coupling here.\"\"\"\n89|    return set()\n90|\n91|\n92|def verify_clone_identity(\n93|    *, vmid: int, hostname: str, mac: str, ip: str | None,\n94|    expected_agent_id: str, guest_agent_id: str | None,\n95|    other_runtime_ips: set[str],\n96|) -> HygieneReport:\n97|    \"\"\"Run all fail-closed identity checks. Raises HygieneError on any failure.\"\"\"\n98|    rep = HygieneReport(vmid=vmid)\n99|\n100|    # 1. VMID uniqueness is structural (PVE enforces); assert nonzero\n101|    rep.require(\"vmid_valid\", bool(vmid), f\"vmid={vmid}\")\n102|\n103|    # 2. MAC format + non-default (template MAC reuse would collide at L2)\n104|    rep.require(\n105|        \"mac_valid\",\n106|        bool(re.fullmatch(r\"([0-9A-Fa-f]{2}:){5}[0-9A-Fa-f]{2}\", mac or \"\"))\n107|        and not mac.upper().startswith(\"BC:24:11:00\"),\n108|        f\"mac={mac} (template-default or malformed MACs rejected)\",\n109|    )\n110|\n111|    # 3. hostname set and template-name not inherited\n112|    rep.require(\n113|        \"hostname_reidentified\",\n114|        bool(hostname) and not hostname.lower().startswith(\"template\"),\n115|        f\"hostname={hostname}\",\n116|    )\n117|\n118|    # 4. IP claim checks — authoritative inventory + other runtimes' IPs\n119|    if ip:\n120|        claimed = _lease_inventory() | set(other_runtime_ips)\n121|        # The candidate IP itself may appear in leases if this VM already leased\n122|        # it (its own claim is fine). Callers may pass ip=mac mapping; here we\n123|        # conservatively require: no OTHER runtime holds it.\n124|        rep.require(\n125|            \"ip_not_owned_by_other_runtime\",\n126|            ip not in other_runtime_ips,\n127|            f\"ip={ip} already bound to another runtime's metadata\",\n128|        )\n129|\n130|        # 5. THE VM108/VM124 LESSON: no inherited conflicting STATIC netplan.\n131|        # A template-carried static config for an IP != the intended one means\n132|        # the guest will fight for the wrong address after reboot.\n133|        guest_statics = _guest_netplan_static_ips(\"\", vmid)\n134|        bad = [g for g in guest_statics if g != ip]\n135|        rep.require(\n136|            \"netplan_reidentified\",\n137|            not bad,\n138|            f\"guest still holds static netplan address(es) {bad} != intended {ip} \"\n139|            \"(shared-template clone not re-identified; fix netplan before READY)\",\n140|        )\n141|\n142|    # 6. Bridge/guest identity matches ARM metadata\n143|    rep.require(\n144|        \"guest_identity_matches\",\n145|        guest_agent_id == expected_agent_id,\n146|        f\"guest reports agent_id={guest_agent_id!r}, ARM expects {expected_agent_id!r}\",\n147|    )\n148|\n149|    return rep\n150|", "total_lines": 149, "file_size": 5950, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

Finish and verify Phase 0 before beginning any Agent Manager implementation work.  \n**Primary objective:** First close the remaining LLM Manager v0.11 baseline work and formally establish the default model baseline. Then execute the complete Agent Manager V2 implementation plan on the same VM114 application/infrastructure.  \n\n---\n\n# MANDATORY EXECUTION ORDER\n\nThis document contains two sequential bodies of work. **Do not interleave them.**\n\n1. **PHASE 0 — CLOSE MARION-IA-USA LLM MANAGER v0.11.** Execute the incorporated handoff below in the stated queue order. Treat the handoff's verified live state as the starting point, but re-verify before changing anything because runtime state can drift.\n2. **PHASE 0 EXIT GATE.** Do not start Agent Manager work until:\n   - the MIAM-00111 production topology is selected, implemented, verified, and documented;\n   - the required remaining MIAM-00111 benchmark legs are complete;\n   - the Phase 21 frontier-pool work is either completed or explicitly documented as BLOCKED/DEFERRED according to the handoff constraints;\n   - `MARION-IA-USA-DEFAULT-MODEL-BASELINE-QUALIFICATION.md` is written;\n   - the §24 final baseline acceptance gate is formally ticked and the baseline is marked **ESTABLISHED**;\n   - the requested cleanup/validation items are completed or explicitly documented with rationale if optional/deferred;\n   - LLM Manager services are healthy and the working Worker Bee service remains available.\n3. **PHASE 1+ — AGENT MANAGER.** After the Phase 0 exit gate passes, continue directly into the full original Agent Manager V2 plan included after the handoff. Do not stop merely because Phase 0 completed.\n\n## Phase 0 operating rules\n\n- The uploaded handoff is the authoritative queue for remaining v0.11 work and explicitly supersedes the older §7 queue in `HANDOVER-v011.md`.\n- Preserve the handoff's constraints exactly, especially: no RDMA/multi-node/SR-IOV work for Phase 21, do not disturb the Worker Bee rollout, preserve rollback presets, do not expose secrets, and use the established branch/repository authorization rules.\n- Prefer the handoff recommendation of **TP2/DP3** for MIAM-00111 because the recorded evidence strongly favors it at the ~10-concurrent-coding-agent target. Human decisions now resolved\n\nThese decisions are authoritative for this execution window.\n\n## 1.1 Credential rotation — DEFER\n\n**Decision:** Do **not** rotate credentials now.\n\nReason:\n\n```text\nno active security threat has been identified\nrotation is useful future maintenance\nit is not urgent enough to interrupt current engineering work\n```\n\nRequired action:\n\n- leave the existing credential-rotation runbook intact;\n- keep credential rotation in Future Work / technical debt / Human Attention;\n- do not rotate or revoke current Bridge/LLM worker credentials in this sprint;\n- do not repeatedly raise it as an urgent blocker unless new evidence of compromise appears.\n\nIf an actual compromise or active threat is discovered, stop and escalate.\n\n---\n\n## 1.2 Branch protection — APPROVED\n\n**Decision:** Protect every repository/branch that AI agents actively modify.\n\nRequired policy for protected `main`/production branches:\n\n```text\npull request required\nrequired CI/status checks must pass\nnormal AI-agent identities may NOT bypass protection\nnormal AI-agent work must NOT push directly to main\nautomated AI PR creation/review/merge is allowed\nhuman approval is NOT required on every PR\nhuman administrators retain emergency/direct-main authority\nforce-push disabled for normal agent identities\nbranch deletion disabled for protected production branches\n```\n\nImportant intent:\n\n> Branch protection exists to prevent accidental direct pushes, not to force a human to click “Approve” for every autonomous change.\n\nAI agents may continue the successful current workflow:\n\n```text\nbranch\n→ implementation\n→ tests\n→ PR\n→ required checks\n→ automated/self review\n→ merge when green\n→ safe deployment\n```\n\nHuman administrators may bypass in emergencies or intentionally commit directly when necessary.\n\n---\n\n## 1.3 GPU telemetry retention — APPROVED\n\nUse the previously proposed **Option A**.\n\n### Raw data\n\n```text\ngpu_samples raw retention: 90 days\n```\n\n### Aggregated data\n\n```text\nhourly summaries retention: 2 years\n```\n\n### Partitioning\n\nPostgreSQL partitioning is **deferred** until database size, ingest rate, or query performance demonstrates a real need.\n\nRecord partitioning as technical debt / future scaling work.\n\n### Pruning authorization\n\nAutomatic pruning of raw `gpu_samples` older than 90 days is authorized **only after**:\n\n```text\nhourly rollup table exists\nbackfill completes\naggregation is verified against raw samples\nbackup/recovery point exists\nretention is configuration-backed\ndry-run pruning result is reviewed programmatically\nrollback procedure exists\n```\n\nNever prune first and aggregate later.\n\n---\n\n## 1.4 ADR-0011 — ACCEPTED\n\n**ADR-0011 is accepted.**\n\nCanonical work semantics:\n\n```text\none ACMS Work Item\n=\none measurable goal with a measurable deliverable\n```\n\nA Work Item may contain:\n\n```text\nmultiple execution sessions\nmultiple /new context rotations\nmultiple tasks\nmultiple worker agents\nmultiple commits\nmultiple PRs when necessary\n```\n\nprovided they all contribute to the same measurable goal.\n\nA `/new` caused by context size, model/session rotation, or interruption does **not** create a new Work Item if the measurable goal remains unchanged.\n\nWhen the measurable goal changes:\n\n```text\ncreate a new Work Item\n```\n\nWork Items may share a higher-level:\n\n```text\nJira issue\nProject\nFeature\nProgram\nhuman business goal\n```\n\n### Work creation authority\n\n**Executive Agent:**\n\n```text\nMAY create additional ACMS Work Items\nMAY decompose an approved higher-level goal\nMAY assign/delegate those Work Items\nMAY create internal tasks/assignments\nMUST stay within approved scope/budget\n```\n\n**Subordinate worker agents:**\n\n```text\nMAY execute assigned work\nMAY create implementation subtasks/checklists inside their Work Item\nMAY produce handoffs/recommend next work\nMAY NOT autonomously create new ACMS Work Items\nMAY NOT silently expand scope\n```\n\nIf a worker identifies new work:\n\n```text\nworker records recommendation in handoff\n→ Executive Agent decides whether to create a new Work Item\n```\n\nThis is intended to prevent recursive/unbounded work creation.\n\n---\n\n## 1.5 Jira / ACMS integration — APPROVED DIRECTION\n\nJira remains the **human-facing planning system**.\n\nACMS is the **AI execution/orchestration system beneath it**.\n\nCanonical relationship:\n\n```text\nHuman\n→ Jira issue / Epic / Task\n→ assigned conceptually to Executive Agent\n→ ACMS creates internal Work Items\n→ Executive Agent delegates to workers\n→ ACMS tracks detailed execution\n→ summaries / links / useful status flow back to Jira\n```\n\n### Jira ticket policy\n\nDo **not** create a Jira ticket for every AI microtask.\n\nDo **not** create “bazillions” of Jira issues corresponding to internal Work Items.\n\nInstead:\n\n```text\n1 Jira item\n→ potentially many ACMS Work Items/tasks\n```\n\nACMS internal decomposition stays internal.\n\n### Jira updates allowed\n\nThe integration may automatically:\n\n```text\nread Jira work assigned to the designated AI/Executive account\nread Jira issue descriptions/comments/context\nattach ACMS/Jira correlation IDs\nadd useful status comments\nadd handoff/result summaries\nadd links to artifacts/PRs\nreport completion/blockers\n```\n\nFor v1, avoid complex bidirectional workflow automation unless necessary.\n\nDo not automatically create child Jira tickets for ACMS work.\n\nDo not build Linear as the primary integration.\n\n### Jira vs Linear\n\nCurrent direction:\n\n```text\nJira = primary human work-management system\nLinear = existing synchronized secondary system / fallback\n```\n\nReason:\n\n- Jira supports software and non-software work;\n- Jira is highly customizable;\n- Linear is primarily software-oriented;\n- required Linear features would add unnecessary cost;\n- Startup Teams already has Jira↔Linear synchronization if Linear becomes useful later.\n\nDo not spend engineering time replacing Jira with Linear.\n\n### Jira account\n\nA dedicated full Jira account already exists for:\n\n```text\nmiamtechnologypvtltd@gmail.com\n```\n\nThe Executive Agent integration may use the authorized Jira account/service identity to:\n\n```text\nsee work assigned by humans\nread issue context\npost comments/status summaries\nlink ACMS execution results\n```\n\nNever place Jira passwords, API tokens, recovery codes, or session cookies in Git.\n\nPrefer API token/OAuth/service authentication over interactive browser login where supported.\n\n---\n\n## 1.6 `agentifyme-external-agent-base` — CREATION APPROVED\n\nCreate a **private** Human decisions now resolved\n\nThese decisions are authoritative for this execution window.\n\n## 1.1 Credential rotation — DEFER\n\n**Decision:** Do **not** rotate credentials now.\n\nReason:\n\n```text\nno active security threat has been identified\nrotation is useful future maintenance\nit is not urgent enough to interrupt current engineering work\n```\n\nRequired action:\n\n- leave the existing credential-rotation runbook intact;\n- keep credential rotation in Future Work / technical debt / Human Attention;\n- do not rotate or revoke current Bridge/LLM worker credentials in this sprint;\n- do not repeatedly raise it as an urgent blocker unless new evidence of compromise appears.\n\nIf an actual compromise or active threat is discovered, stop and escalate.\n\n---\n\n## 1.2 Branch protection — APPROVED\n\n**Decision:** Protect every repository/branch that AI agents actively modify.\n\nRequired policy for protected `main`/production branches:\n\n```text\npull request required\nrequired CI/status checks must pass\nnormal AI-agent identities may NOT bypass protection\nnormal AI-agent work must NOT push directly to main\nautomated AI PR creation/review/merge is allowed\nhuman approval is NOT required on every PR\nhuman administrators retain emergency/direct-main authority\nforce-push disabled for normal agent identities\nbranch deletion disabled for protected production branches\n```\n\nImportant intent:\n\n> Branch protection exists to prevent accidental direct pushes, not to force a human to click “Approve” for every autonomous change.\n\nAI agents may continue the successful current workflow:\n\n```text\nbranch\n→ implementation\n→ tests\n→ PR\n→ required checks\n→ automated/self review\n→ merge when green\n→ safe deployment\n```\n\nHuman administrators may bypass in emergencies or intentionally commit directly when necessary.\n\n---\n\n## 1.3 GPU telemetry retention — APPROVED\n\nUse the previously proposed **Option A**.\n\n### Raw data\n\n```text\ngpu_samples raw retention: 90 days\n```\n\n### Aggregated data\n\n```text\nhourly summaries retention: 2 years\n```\n\n### Partitioning\n\nPostgreSQL partitioning is **deferred** until database size, ingest rate, or query performance demonstrates a real need.\n\nRecord partitioning as technical debt / future scaling work.\n\n### Pruning authorization\n\nAutomatic pruning of raw `gpu_samples` older than 90 days is authorized **only after**:\n\n```text\nhourly rollup table exists\nbackfill completes\naggregation is verified against raw samples\nbackup/recovery point exists\nretention is configuration-backed\ndry-run pruning result is reviewed programmatically\nrollback procedure exists\n```\n\nNever prune first and aggregate later.\n\n---\n\n## 1.4 ADR-0011 — ACCEPTED\n\n**ADR-0011 is accepted.**\n\nCanonical work semantics:\n\n```text\none ACMS Work Item\n=\none measurable goal with a measurable deliverable\n```\n\nA Work Item may contain:\n\n```text\nmultiple execution sessions\nmultiple /new context rotations\nmultiple tasks\nmultiple worker agents\nmultiple commits\nmultiple PRs when necessary\n```\n\nprovided they all contribute to the same measurable goal.\n\nA `/new` caused by context size, model/session rotation, or interruption does **not** create a new Work Item if the measurable goal remains unchanged.\n\nWhen the measurable goal changes:\n\n```text\ncreate a new Work Item\n```\n\nWork Items may share a higher-level:\n\n```text\nJira issue\nProject\nFeature\nProgram\nhuman business goal\n```\n\n### Work creation authority\n\n**Executive Agent:**\n\n```text\nMAY create additional ACMS Work Items\nMAY decompose an approved higher-level goal\nMAY assign/delegate those Work Items\nMAY create internal tasks/assignments\nMUST stay within approved scope/budget\n```\n\n**Subordinate worker agents:**\n\n```text\nMAY execute assigned work\nMAY create implementation subtasks/checklists inside their Work Item\nMAY produce handoffs/recommend next work\nMAY NOT autonomously create new ACMS Work Items\nMAY NOT silently expand scope\n```\n\nIf a worker identifies new work:\n\n```text\nworker records recommendation in handoff\n→ Executive Agent decides whether to create a new Work Item\n```\n\nThis is intended to prevent recursive/unbounded work creation.\n\n---\n\n## 1.5 Jira / ACMS integration — APPROVED DIRECTION\n\nJira remains the **human-facing planning system**.\n\nACMS is the **AI execution/orchestration system beneath it**.\n\nCanonical relationship:\n\n```text\nHuman\n→ Jira issue / Epic / Task\n→ assigned conceptually to Executive Agent\n→ ACMS creates internal Work Items\n→ Executive Agent delegates to workers\n→ ACMS tracks detailed execution\n→ summaries / links / useful status flow back to Jira\n```\n\n### Jira ticket policy\n\nDo **not** create a Jira ticket for every AI microtask.\n\nDo **not** create “bazillions” of Jira issues corresponding to internal Work Items.\n\nInstead:\n\n```text\n1 Jira item\n→ potentially many ACMS Work Items/tasks\n```\n\nACMS internal decomposition stays internal.\n\n### Jira updates allowed\n\nThe integration may automatically:\n\n```text\nread Jira work assigned to the designated AI/Executive account\nread Jira issue descriptions/comments/context\nattach ACMS/Jira correlation IDs\nadd useful status comments\nadd handoff/result summaries\nadd links to artifacts/PRs\nreport completion/blockers\n```\n\nFor v1, avoid complex bidirectional workflow automation unless necessary.\n\nDo not automatically create child Jira tickets for ACMS work.\n\nDo not build Linear as the primary integration.\n\n### Jira vs Linear\n\nCurrent direction:\n\n```text\nJira = primary human work-management system\nLinear = existing synchronized secondary system / fallback\n```\n\nReason:\n\n- Jira supports software and non-software work;\n- Jira is highly customizable;\n- Linear is primarily software-oriented;\n- required Linear features would add unnecessary cost;\n- Startup Teams already has Jira↔Linear synchronization if Linear becomes useful later.\n\nDo not spend engineering time replacing Jira with Linear.\n\n### Jira account\n\nA dedicated full Jira account already exists for:\n\n```text\nmiamtechnologypvtltd@gmail.com\n```\n\nThe Executive Agent integration may use the authorized Jira account/service identity to:\n\n```text\nsee work assigned by humans\nread issue context\npost comments/status summaries\nlink ACMS execution results\n```\n\nNever place Jira passwords, API tokens, recovery codes, or session cookies in Git.\n\nPrefer API token/OAuth/service authentication over interactive browser login where supported.\n\n---\n\n## 1.6 `agentifyme-external-agent-base` — CREATION APPROVED\n\nCreate a **private** repository:\n\n```text\nstartupteams/agentifyme-external-agent-base\n```\n\nPurpose:\n\n> Temporary safe baseline for external/customer-facing AgentifyMe workers, including Speed-to-Lead Human decisions now resolved\n\nThese decisions are authoritative for this execution window.\n\n## 1.1 Credential rotation — DEFER\n\n**Decision:** Do **not** rotate credentials now.\n\nReason:\n\n```text\nno active security threat has been identified\nrotation is useful future maintenance\nit is not urgent enough to interrupt current engineering work\n```\n\nRequired action:\n\n- leave the existing credential-rotation runbook intact;\n- keep credential rotation in Future Work / technical debt / Human Attention;\n- do not rotate or revoke current Bridge/LLM worker credentials in this sprint;\n- do not repeatedly raise it as an urgent blocker unless new evidence of compromise appears.\n\nIf an actual compromise or active threat is discovered, stop and escalate.\n\n---\n\n## 1.2 Branch protection — APPROVED\n\n**Decision:** Protect every repository/branch that AI agents actively modify.\n\nRequired policy for protected `main`/production branches:\n\n```text\npull request required\nrequired CI/status checks must pass\nnormal AI-agent identities may NOT bypass protection\nnormal AI-agent work must NOT push directly to main\nautomated AI PR creation/review/merge is allowed\nhuman approval is NOT required on every PR\nhuman administrators retain emergency/direct-main authority\nforce-push disabled for normal agent identities\nbranch deletion disabled for protected production branches\n```\n\nImportant intent:\n\n> Branch protection exists to prevent accidental direct pushes, not to force a human to click “Approve” for every autonomous change.\n\nAI agents may continue the successful current workflow:\n\n```text\nbranch\n→ implementation\n→ tests\n→ PR\n→ required checks\n→ automated/self review\n→ merge when green\n→ safe deployment\n```\n\nHuman administrators may bypass in emergencies or intentionally commit directly when necessary.\n\n---\n\n## 1.3 GPU telemetry retention — APPROVED\n\nUse the previously proposed **Option A**.\n\n### Raw data\n\n```text\ngpu_samples raw retention: 90 days\n```\n\n### Aggregated data\n\n```text\nhourly summaries retention: 2 years\n```\n\n### Partitioning\n\nPostgreSQL partitioning is **deferred** until database size, ingest rate, or query performance demonstrates a real need.\n\nRecord partitioning as technical debt / future scaling work.\n\n### Pruning authorization\n\nAutomatic pruning of raw `gpu_samples` older than 90 days is authorized **only after**:\n\n```text\nhourly rollup table exists\nbackfill completes\naggregation is verified against raw samples\nbackup/recovery point exists\nretention is configuration-backed\ndry-run pruning result is reviewed programmatically\nrollback procedure exists\n```\n\nNever prune first and aggregate later.\n\n---\n\n## 1.4 ADR-0011 — ACCEPTED\n\n**ADR-0011 is accepted.**\n\nCanonical work semantics:\n\n```text\none ACMS Work Item\n=\none measurable goal with a measurable deliverable\n```\n\nA Work Item may contain:\n\n```text\nmultiple execution sessions\nmultiple /new context rotations\nmultiple tasks\nmultiple worker agents\nmultiple commits\nmultiple PRs when necessary\n```\n\nprovided they all contribute to the same measurable goal.\n\nA `/new` caused by context size, model/session rotation, or interruption does **not** create a new Work Item if the measurable goal remains unchanged.\n\nWhen the measurable goal changes:\n\n```text\ncreate a new Work Item\n```\n\nWork Items may share a higher-level:\n\n```text\nJira issue\nProject\nFeature\nProgram\nhuman business goal\n```\n\n### Work creation authority\n\n**Executive Agent:**\n\n```text\nMAY create additional ACMS Work Items\nMAY decompose an approved higher-level goal\nMAY assign/delegate those Work Items\nMAY create internal tasks/assignments\nMUST stay within approved scope/budget\n```\n\n**Subordinate worker agents:**\n\n```text\nMAY execute assigned work\nMAY create implementation subtasks/checklists inside their Work Item\nMAY produce handoffs/recommend next work\nMAY NOT autonomously create new ACMS Work Items\nMAY NOT silently expand scope\n```\n\nIf a worker identifies new work:\n\n```text\nworker records recommendation in handoff\n→ Executive Agent decides whether to create a new Work Item\n```\n\nThis is intended to prevent recursive/unbounded work creation.\n\n---\n\n## 1.5 Jira / ACMS integration — APPROVED DIRECTION\n\nJira remains the **human-facing planning system**.\n\nACMS is the **AI execution/orchestration system beneath it**.\n\nCanonical relationship:\n\n```text\nHuman\n→ Jira issue / Epic / Task\n→ assigned conceptually to Executive Agent\n→ ACMS creates internal Work Items\n→ Executive Agent delegates to workers\n→ ACMS tracks detailed execution\n→ summaries / links / useful status flow back to Jira\n```\n\n### Jira ticket policy\n\nDo **not** create a Jira ticket for every AI microtask.\n\nDo **not** create “bazillions” of Jira issues corresponding to internal Work Items.\n\nInstead:\n\n```text\n1 Jira item\n→ potentially many ACMS Work Items/tasks\n```\n\nACMS internal decomposition stays internal.\n\n### Jira updates allowed\n\nThe integration may automatically:\n\n```text\nread Jira work assigned to the designated AI/Executive account\nread Jira issue descriptions/comments/context\nattach ACMS/Jira correlation IDs\nadd useful status comments\nadd handoff/result summaries\nadd links to artifacts/PRs\nreport completion/blockers\n```\n\nFor v1, avoid complex bidirectional workflow automation unless necessary.\n\nDo not automatically create child Jira tickets for ACMS work.\n\nDo not build Linear as the primary integration.\n\n### Jira vs Linear\n\nCurrent direction:\n\n```text\nJira = primary human work-management system\nLinear = existing synchronized secondary system / fallback\n```\n\nReason:\n\n- Jira supports software and non-software work;\n- Jira is highly customizable;\n- Linear is primarily software-oriented;\n- required Linear features would add unnecessary cost;\n- Startup Teams already has Jira↔Linear synchronization if Linear becomes useful later.\n\nDo not spend engineering time replacing Jira with Linear.\n\n### Jira account\n\nA dedicated full Jira account already exists for:\n\n```text\nmiamtechnologypvtltd@gmail.com\n```\n\nThe Executive Agent integration may use the authorized Jira account/service identity to:\n\n```text\nsee work assigned by humans\nread issue context\npost comments/status summaries\nlink ACMS execution results\n```\n\nNever place Jira passwords, API tokens, recovery codes, or session cookies in Git.\n\nPrefer API token/OAuth/service Human decisions now resolved\n\nThese decisions are authoritative for this execution window.\n\n## 1.1 Credential rotation — DEFER\n\n**Decision:** Do **not** rotate credentials now.\n\nReason:\n\n```text\nno active security threat has been identified\nrotation is useful future maintenance\nit is not urgent enough to interrupt current engineering work\n```\n\nRequired action:\n\n- leave the existing credential-rotation runbook intact;\n- keep credential rotation in Future Work / technical debt / Human Attention;\n- do not rotate or revoke current Bridge/LLM worker credentials in this sprint;\n- do not repeatedly raise it as an urgent blocker unless new evidence of compromise appears.\n\nIf an actual compromise or active threat is discovered, stop and escalate.\n\n---\n\n## 1.2 Branch protection — APPROVED\n\n**Decision:** Protect every repository/branch that AI agents actively modify.\n\nRequired policy for protected `main`/production branches:\n\n```text\npull request required\nrequired CI/status checks must pass\nnormal AI-agent identities may NOT bypass protection\nnormal AI-agent work must NOT push directly to main\nautomated AI PR creation/review/merge is allowed\nhuman approval is NOT required on every PR\nhuman administrators retain emergency/direct-main authority\nforce-push disabled for normal agent identities\nbranch deletion disabled for protected production branches\n```\n\nImportant intent:\n\n> Branch protection exists to prevent accidental direct pushes, not to force a human to click “Approve” for every autonomous change.\n\nAI agents may continue the successful current workflow:\n\n```text\nbranch\n→ implementation\n→ tests\n→ PR\n→ required checks\n→ automated/self review\n→ merge when green\n→ safe deployment\n```\n\nHuman administrators may bypass in emergencies or intentionally commit directly when necessary.\n\n---\n\n## 1.3 GPU telemetry retention — APPROVED\n\nUse the previously proposed **Option A**.\n\n### Raw data\n\n```text\ngpu_samples raw retention: 90 days\n```\n\n### Aggregated data\n\n```text\nhourly summaries retention: 2 years\n```\n\n### Partitioning\n\nPostgreSQL partitioning is **deferred** until database size, ingest rate, or query performance demonstrates a real need.\n\nRecord partitioning as technical debt / future scaling work.\n\n### Pruning authorization\n\nAutomatic pruning of raw `gpu_samples` older than 90 days is authorized **only after**:\n\n```text\nhourly rollup table exists\nbackfill completes\naggregation is verified against raw samples\nbackup/recovery point exists\nretention is configuration-backed\ndry-run pruning result is reviewed programmatically\nrollback procedure exists\n```\n\nNever prune first and aggregate later.\n\n---\n\n## 1.4 ADR-0011 — ACCEPTED\n\n**ADR-0011 is accepted.**\n\nCanonical work semantics:\n\n```text\none ACMS Work Item\n=\none measurable goal with a measurable deliverable\n```\n\nA Work Item may contain:\n\n```text\nmultiple execution sessions\nmultiple /new context rotations\nmultiple tasks\nmultiple worker agents\nmultiple commits\nmultiple PRs when necessary\n```\n\nprovided they all contribute to the same measurable goal.\n\nA `/new` caused by context size, model/session rotation, or interruption does **not** create a new Work Item if the measurable goal remains unchanged.\n\nWhen the measurable goal changes:\n\n```text\ncreate a new Work Item\n```\n\nWork Items may share a higher-level:\n\n```text\nJira issue\nProject\nFeature\nProgram\nhuman business goal\n```\n\n### Work creation authority\n\n**Executive Agent:**\n\n```text\nMAY create additional ACMS Work Items\nMAY decompose an approved higher-level goal\nMAY assign/delegate those Work Items\nMAY create internal tasks/assignments\nMUST stay within approved scope/budget\n```\n\n**Subordinate worker agents:**\n\n```text\nMAY execute assigned work\nMAY create implementation subtasks/checklists inside their Work Item\nMAY produce handoffs/recommend next work\nMAY NOT autonomously create new ACMS Work Items\nMAY NOT silently expand scope\n```\n\nIf a worker identifies new work:\n\n```text\nworker records recommendation in handoff\n→ Executive Agent decides whether to create a new Work Item\n```\n\nThis is intended to prevent recursive/unbounded work creation.\n\n---\n\n## 1.5 Jira / ACMS integration — APPROVED DIRECTION\n\nJira remains the **human-facing planning system**.\n\nACMS is the **AI execution/orchestration system beneath it**.\n\nCanonical relationship:\n\n```text\nHuman\n→ Jira issue / Epic / Task\n→ assigned conceptually to Executive Agent\n→ ACMS creates internal Work Items\n→ Executive Agent delegates to workers\n→ ACMS tracks detailed execution\n→ summaries / links / useful status flow back to Jira\n```\n\n### Jira ticket policy\n\nDo **not** create a Jira ticket for every AI microtask.\n\nDo **not** create “bazillions” of Jira issues corresponding to internal Work Items.\n\nInstead:\n\n```text\n1 Jira item\n→ potentially many ACMS Work Items/tasks\n```\n\nACMS internal decomposition stays internal.\n\n### Jira updates allowed\n\nThe integration may automatically:\n\n```text\nread Jira work assigned to the designated AI/Executive account\nread Jira issue descriptions/comments/context\nattach ACMS/Jira correlation IDs\nadd useful status comments\nadd handoff/result summaries\nadd links to artifacts/PRs\nreport completion/blockers\n```\n\nFor v1, avoid complex bidirectional workflow automation unless necessary.\n\nDo not automatically create child Jira tickets for ACMS work.\n\nDo not build Linear as the primary integration.\n\n### Jira vs Linear\n\nCurrent direction:\n\n```text\nJira = primary human work-management system\nLinear = existing synchronized secondary system / fallback\n```\n\nReason:\n\n- Jira supports software and non-software work;\n- Jira is highly customizable;\n- Linear is primarily software-oriented;\n- required Linear features would add unnecessary cost;\n- Startup Teams already has Jira↔Linear synchronization if Linear becomes useful later.\n\nDo not spend engineering time replacing Jira with Linear.\n\n### Jira account\n\nA dedicated full Jira account already exists for:\n\n```text\nmiamtechnologypvtltd@gmail.com\n```\n\nThe Executive Agent integration may use the authorized Jira account/service identity to:\n\n```text\nsee work assigned by humans\nread issue Human decisions now resolved\n\nThese decisions are authoritative for this execution window.\n\n## 1.1 Credential rotation — DEFER\n\n**Decision:** Do **not** rotate credentials now.\n\nReason:\n\n```text\nno active security threat has been identified\nrotation is useful future maintenance\nit is not urgent enough to interrupt current engineering work\n```\n\nRequired action:\n\n- leave the existing credential-rotation runbook intact;\n- keep credential rotation in Future Work / technical debt / Human Attention;\n- do not rotate or revoke current Bridge/LLM worker credentials in this sprint;\n- do not repeatedly raise it as an urgent blocker unless new evidence of compromise appears.\n\nIf an actual compromise or active threat is discovered, stop and escalate.\n\n---\n\n## 1.2 Branch protection — APPROVED\n\n**Decision:** Protect every repository/branch that AI agents actively modify.\n\nRequired policy for protected `main`/production branches:\n\n```text\npull request required\nrequired CI/status checks must pass\nnormal AI-agent identities may NOT bypass protection\nnormal AI-agent work must NOT push directly to main\nautomated AI PR creation/review/merge is allowed\nhuman approval is NOT required on every PR\nhuman administrators retain emergency/direct-main authority\nforce-push disabled for normal agent identities\nbranch deletion disabled for protected production branches\n```\n\nImportant intent:\n\n> Branch protection exists to prevent accidental direct pushes, not to force a human to click “Approve” for every autonomous change.\n\nAI agents may continue the successful current workflow:\n\n```text\nbranch\n→ implementation\n→ tests\n→ PR\n→ required checks\n→ automated/self review\n→ merge when green\n→ safe deployment\n```\n\nHuman administrators may bypass in emergencies or intentionally commit directly when necessary.\n\n---\n\n## 1.3 GPU telemetry retention — APPROVED\n\nUse the previously proposed **Option A**.\n\n### Raw data\n\n```text\ngpu_samples raw retention: 90 days\n```\n\n### Aggregated data\n\n```text\nhourly summaries retention: 2 years\n```\n\n### Partitioning\n\nPostgreSQL partitioning is **deferred** until database size, ingest rate, or query performance demonstrates a real need.\n\nRecord partitioning as technical debt / future scaling work.\n\n### Pruning authorization\n\nAutomatic pruning of raw `gpu_samples` older than 90 days is authorized **only after**:\n\n```text\nhourly rollup table exists\nbackfill completes\naggregation is verified against raw samples\nbackup/recovery point exists\nretention is configuration-backed\ndry-run pruning result is reviewed programmatically\nrollback procedure exists\n```\n\nNever prune first and aggregate later.\n\n---\n\n## 1.4 ADR-0011 — ACCEPTED\n\n**ADR-0011 is accepted.**\n\nCanonical work semantics:\n\n```text\none ACMS Work Item\n=\none measurable goal with a measurable deliverable\n```\n\nA Work Item may contain:\n\n```text\nmultiple execution sessions\nmultiple /new context rotations\nmultiple tasks\nmultiple worker agents\nmultiple commits\nmultiple PRs when necessary\n```\n\nprovided they all contribute to the same measurable goal.\n\nA `/new` caused by context size, model/session rotation, or interruption does **not** create a new Work Item if the measurable goal remains unchanged.\n\nWhen the measurable goal changes:\n\n```text\ncreate a new Work Item\n```\n\nWork Items may share a higher-level:\n\n```text\nJira issue\nProject\nFeature\nProgram\nhuman business goal\n```\n\n### Work creation authority\n\n**Executive Agent:**\n\n```text\nMAY create additional ACMS Work Items\nMAY decompose an approved higher-level goal\nMAY assign/delegate those Work Items\nMAY create internal tasks/assignments\nMUST stay within approved scope/budget\n```\n\n**Subordinate worker agents:**\n\n```text\nMAY execute assigned work\nMAY create implementation subtasks/checklists inside their Work Item\nMAY produce handoffs/recommend next work\nMAY NOT autonomously create new ACMS Work Items\nMAY NOT silently expand scope\n```\n\nIf a worker identifies new work:\n\n```text\nworker records recommendation in handoff\n→ Executive Agent decides whether to create a new Work Item\n```\n\nThis is intended to prevent recursive/unbounded work creation.\n\n---\n\n## 1.5 Jira / ACMS integration — APPROVED DIRECTION\n\nJira remains the **human-facing planning system**.\n\nACMS is the **AI execution/orchestration system beneath it**.\n\nCanonical relationship:\n\n```text\nHuman\n→ Jira issue / Epic / Task\n→ assigned conceptually to Executive Agent\n→ ACMS creates internal Work Items\n→ Executive Agent delegates to workers\n→ ACMS tracks detailed execution\n→ summaries / links / useful status flow back to Jira\n```\n\n### Jira ticket policy\n\nDo **not** create a Jira ticket for every AI microtask.\n\nDo **not** create “bazillions” of Jira issues corresponding to internal Work Items.\n\nInstead:\n\n```text\n1 Jira item\n→ potentially many ACMS Work Items/tasks\n```\n\nACMS internal decomposition stays internal.\n\n### Jira updates allowed\n\nThe integration may automatically:\n\n```text\nread Jira work assigned to the designated AI/Executive account\nread Jira issue descriptions/comments/context\nattach ACMS/Jira correlation IDs\nadd useful status comments\nadd handoff/result summaries\nadd links to artifacts/PRs\nreport completion/blockers\n```\n\nFor v1, avoid complex bidirectional workflow automation unless necessary.\n\nDo not automatically create child Jira tickets for ACMS work.\n\nDo not build Linear as the primary integration.\n\n### Jira vs Linear\n\nCurrent direction:\n\n```text\nJira = primary human work-management system\nLinear = existing synchronized secondary system / fallback\n```\n\nReason:\n\n- Jira supports software and non-software work;\n- Jira is highly customizable;\n- Linear is primarily software-oriented;\n- required Linear features would add unnecessary cost;\n- Startup Teams already has Jira↔Linear synchronization if Linear becomes useful later.\n\nDo not spend engineering time replacing Jira with Linear.\n\n### Jira account\n\nA dedicated full Jira account already exists for:\n\n```text\nmiamtechnologypvtltd@gmail.com\n```\n\nThe Executive Agent integration may use the authorized Jira account/service identity to:\n\n```text\nsee work assigned by humans\nread issue context\npost comments/status summaries\nlink ACMS execution results\n```\n\nNever place Jira passwords, API tokens, recovery codes, or session cookies in Git.\n\nPrefer API token/OAuth/service authentication over interactive browser login where supported.\n\n---\n\n## 1.6 `agentifyme-external-agent-base` — CREATION APPROVED\n\nCreate a **private** repository:\n\n```text\nstartupteams/agentifyme-external-agent-base\n```\n\nPurpose:\n\n> Temporary safe baseline for external/customer-facing AgentifyMe workers, including Speed-to-Lead workers.\n\nThis is explicitly a **temporary architecture**, not necessarily the final long-term Agent State solution.\n\n### Allowed reuse from `hermes-base-setup`\n\nSanitized generic mechanisms may be copied/adapted:\n\n```text\n.gitignore strategy\nsecret scanning\nsafe bootstrap mechanics\nversion/build metadata\nstate-sync patterns\ngeneric deployment helpers\ngeneric documentation structure\n```\n\n### Explicitly forbidden content\n\nDo **not** copy:\n\n```text\nStartup Teams internal profiles\nSOUL/persona files containing internal company context\ninternal memories\ninternal skills\ncompany-only instructions\ninternal customer information\nAPI keys/tokens/secrets\nemployee-specific context\nprivate operational notes\n```\n\n### One-time contamination validation\n\nDuring repository creation:\n\n1. create the sanitized baseline in a temporary worktree/directory;\n2. scan it for known internal profile/memory/skill markers;\n3. inspect Git history/content before first production use;\n4. fail the setup if internal Startup Teams content is found;\n5. correct the source tree before use.\n\nA permanent continuous “internal marker” test is **not required** at this time.\n\nNormal secret scanning remains required.\n\n### Speed-to-Lead\n\nSpeed-to-Lead worker agents may temporarily use this repository as their default agent baseline.\n\nDo not copy the internal `hermes-base-setup` agent branches into it.\n\n---\n\n# 2. Baseline from latest handoff\n\nDo not redo completed work.\n\nExpected production baseline:\n\n```text\nACMS CT122:\n  6f772aa\n  v0.11.0\n  alembic 0008\n\nLLM Manager / ARM VM114:\n  cb82bcd\n  ORM slices 1–4 live\n  Server Manager API healthy\n\nVM124 / acms-worker-001:\n  healthy\n  Bridge proven\n  real budget-gated dispatch proven\n  state sync VERIFIED\n\nAttention:\n  API + UI + SSE live\n\nRecovery:\n  ARM max 5 attempts\n  ACMS max 3 Executive reassignments\n```\n\nVerify current `main`, production SHA, health, and migrations before modifying anything.\n\nIf production moved forward compatibly, use current main rather than resetting to these SHAs.\n\n---\n\n# 3.
