Inventory repositories AI agents modify\n\nAt minimum inspect:\n\n```text\nstartupteams/acms-project-framework\nstartupteams/llm-manager-project-framework\nstartupteams/pdu-marion-ia-usa-project-framework\nstartupteams/hermes-base-setup\nstartupteams/agentifyme-speed-to-lead\n```\n\nAlso include any other repository currently modified by autonomous AI agents.\n\nDo not blindly modify unrelated archival repositories.\n\n## A2. Protection policy\n\nFor each active production/default branch:\n\n```text\nrequire pull request before merge\nrequire existing CI/status checks\nAI/machine identity cannot push directly to protected branch\nAI/machine identity cannot bypass required checks\nno normal force push\nno normal branch deletion\n```\n\nDo **not** require a human approving review merely to merge routine AI-generated PRs unless that repository already has such a requirement.\n\nPreserve:\n\n```text\nhuman administrator emergency bypass/direct-main capability\n```\n\nDocument who/what is considered:\n\n```text\nhuman admin\nAI agent identity\ndeployment identity\nCI identity\n```\n\n## A3. Verification\n\nPerform a harmless verification:\n\n```text\nAI identity direct push → rejected\nPR with failing required check → cannot merge\nPR with all required checks → automated merge path works\nhuman admin emergency path remains documented/available\n```\n\nDo not intentionally break production to test this.\n\n## A4. Documentation\n\nCreate/update:\n\n```text\ndocs/operations/BRANCH_PROTECTION_AND_AI_MERGE_POLICY.md\n```\n\nCheckpoint.\n\n---\n\n# 4. Phase B — execute approved GPU retention architecture\n\nImplement Option A safely.\n\n## B1. Re-measure before migration\n\nRecord:\n\n```text\nrow count\ntable size\ndatabase size\noldest/newest sample\napprox rows/day\napprox MB/day\nimportant query patterns\n```\n\nDo not assume the last handoff's values are still exact.\n\n## B2. Create hourly rollup\n\nAdd an authoritative aggregate table, such as:\n\n```text\ngpu_samples_hourly\n```\n\nUse the actual existing schema and metrics rather than inventing columns.\n\nAggregation must preserve the operational statistics required by LLM Manager.\n\nTypical concepts may include where supported:\n\n```text\nhost/GPU identity\nhour bucket\nsample_count\nmin/max/avg utilization\nmemory metrics\ntemperature\npower\nother currently queried telemetry\n```\n\n## B3. Backfill\n\nBackfill all existing raw history.\n\nUse a resumable/idempotent strategy if practical.\n\n## B4. Verification\n\nFor representative windows compare raw calculations against rollup rows.\n\nRequire deterministic tolerance rules for numeric aggregation.\n\nNo pruning until verification passes.\n\n## B5. Retention configuration\n\nConfigure:\n\n```text\nraw retention = 90 days\nhourly rollup retention = 730 days / 2 years\n```\n\nValues should be configuration-backed rather than embedded in a one-off SQL script.\n\n## B6. Backup\n\nConfirm recoverable backup/recovery point before first destructive prune.\n\n## B7. Dry-run prune\n\nCalculate exactly:\n\n```text\ncutoff\ncandidate row count\ncandidate size\noldest row after prune\n```\n\nLog the dry-run result.\n\n## B8. Partitioning\n\nDo not partition now.\n\nCreate/retain TDR/Future Work trigger such as:\n\n```text\npartition if:\ntable growth materially accelerates\nretention delete becomes operationally expensive\nquery latency crosses threshold\nmaintenance/vacuum becomes problematic\n```\n\nCheckpoint.\n\n---\n\n# 5. Human decisions now resolved\n\nThese decisions are authoritative for this execution window.\n\n## 1.1 Credential rotation — DEFER\n\n**Decision:** Do **not** rotate credentials now.\n\nReason:\n\n```text\nno active security threat has been identified\nrotation is useful future maintenance\nit is not urgent enough to interrupt current engineering work\n```\n\nRequired action:\n\n- leave the existing credential-rotation runbook intact;\n- keep credential rotation in Future Work / technical debt / Human Attention;\n- do not rotate or revoke current Bridge/LLM worker credentials in this sprint;\n- do not repeatedly raise it as an urgent blocker unless new evidence of compromise appears.\n\nIf an actual compromise or active threat is discovered, stop and escalate.\n\n---\n\n## 1.2 Branch protection — APPROVED\n\n**Decision:** Protect every repository/branch that AI agents actively modify.\n\nRequired policy for protected `main`/production branches:\n\n```text\npull request required\nrequired CI/status checks must pass\nnormal AI-agent identities may NOT bypass protection\nnormal AI-agent work must NOT push directly to main\nautomated AI PR creation/review/merge is allowed\nhuman approval is NOT required on every PR\nhuman administrators retain emergency/direct-main authority\nforce-push disabled for normal agent identities\nbranch deletion disabled for protected production branches\n```\n\nImportant intent:\n\n> Branch protection exists to prevent accidental direct pushes, not to force a human to click “Approve” for every autonomous change.\n\nAI agents may continue the successful current workflow:\n\n```text\nbranch\n→ implementation\n→ tests\n→ PR\n→ required checks\n→ automated/self review\n→ merge when green\n→ safe deployment\n```\n\nHuman administrators may bypass in emergencies or intentionally commit directly when necessary.\n\n---\n\n## 1.3 GPU telemetry retention — APPROVED\n\nUse the previously proposed **Option A**.\n\n### Raw data\n\n```text\ngpu_samples raw retention: 90 days\n```\n\n### Aggregated data\n\n```text\nhourly summaries retention: 2 years\n```\n\n### Partitioning\n\nPostgreSQL partitioning is **deferred** until database size, ingest rate, or query performance demonstrates a real need.\n\nRecord partitioning as technical debt / future scaling work.\n\n### Pruning authorization\n\nAutomatic pruning of raw `gpu_samples` older than 90 days is authorized **only after**:\n\n```text\nhourly rollup table exists\nbackfill completes\naggregation is verified against raw samples\nbackup/recovery point exists\nretention is configuration-backed\ndry-run pruning result is reviewed programmatically\nrollback procedure exists\n```\n\nNever prune first and aggregate later.\n\n---\n\n## 1.4 ADR-0011 — ACCEPTED\n\n**ADR-0011 is accepted.**\n\nCanonical work semantics:\n\n```text\none ACMS Work Item\n=\none measurable goal with a measurable deliverable\n```\n\nA Work Item may contain:\n\n```text\nmultiple execution sessions\nmultiple /new context rotations\nmultiple tasks\nmultiple worker agents\nmultiple commits\nmultiple PRs when necessary\n```\n\nprovided they all contribute to the same measurable goal.\n\nA `/new` caused by context size, model/session rotation, or interruption does **not** create a new Work Item if the measurable goal remains unchanged.\n\nWhen the measurable goal changes:\n\n```text\ncreate a new Work Item\n```\n\nWork Items may share a higher-level:\n\n```text\nJira issue\nProject\nFeature\nProgram\nhuman business goal\n```\n\n### Work creation authority\n\n**Executive Agent:**\n\n```text\nMAY create additional ACMS Work Items\nMAY decompose an approved higher-level goal\nMAY assign/delegate those Work Items\nMAY create internal tasks/assignments\nMUST stay within approved scope/budget\n```\n\n**Subordinate worker agents:**\n\n```text\nMAY execute assigned work\nMAY create implementation subtasks/checklists inside their Work Item\nMAY produce handoffs/recommend next work\nMAY NOT autonomously create new ACMS Work Items\nMAY NOT silently expand scope\n```\n\nIf a worker identifies new work:\n\n```text\nworker records recommendation in handoff\n→ Executive Agent decides whether to create a new Work Item\n```\n\nThis is intended to prevent recursive/unbounded work creation.\n\n---\n\n## 1.5 Jira / ACMS integration — APPROVED DIRECTION\n\nJira remains the **human-facing planning system**.\n\nACMS is the **AI execution/orchestration system beneath it**.\n\nCanonical relationship:\n\n```text\nHuman\n→ Jira issue / Epic / Task\n→ assigned conceptually to Executive Agent\n→ ACMS creates internal Work Items\n→ Executive Agent delegates to workers\n→ ACMS tracks detailed execution\n→ summaries / links / useful status flow back to Jira\n```\n\n### Jira ticket policy\n\nDo **not** create a Jira ticket for every AI microtask.\n\nDo **not** create “bazillions” of Jira issues corresponding to internal Work Items.\n\nInstead:\n\n```text\n1 Jira item\n→ potentially many ACMS Work Items/tasks\n```\n\nACMS internal decomposition stays internal.\n\n### Jira updates allowed\n\nThe integration may automatically:\n\n```text\nread Jira work assigned to the designated AI/Executive account\nread Jira issue descriptions/comments/context\nattach ACMS/Jira correlation IDs\nadd useful status comments\nadd handoff/result summaries\nadd links to artifacts/PRs\nreport completion/blockers\n```\n\nFor v1, avoid complex bidirectional workflow automation unless necessary.\n\nDo not automatically create child Jira tickets for ACMS work.\n\nDo not build Linear as the primary integration.\n\n### Jira vs Linear\n\nCurrent direction:\n\n```text\nJira = primary human work-management system\nLinear = existing synchronized secondary system / fallback\n```\n\nReason:\n\n- Jira supports software and non-software work;\n- Jira is highly customizable;\n- Linear is primarily software-oriented;\n- required Linear features would add unnecessary cost;\n- Startup Teams already has Jira↔Linear synchronization if Linear becomes useful later.\n\nDo not spend engineering time replacing Jira with Linear.\n\n### Jira account\n\nA dedicated full Jira account already exists for:\n\n```text\nmiamtechnologypvtltd@gmail.com\n```\n\nThe Executive Agent integration may use the authorized Jira account/service identity to:\n\n```text\nsee work assigned by humans\nread issue context\npost comments/status summaries\nlink ACMS execution results\n```\n\nNever place Jira passwords, API tokens, recovery codes, or session cookies in Git.\n\nPrefer API token/OAuth/service authentication over interactive browser login where supported.\n\n---\n\n## 1.6 `agentifyme-external-agent-base` — CREATION APPROVED\n\nCreate a **private** repository:\n\n```text\nstartupteams/agentifyme-external-agent-base\n```\n\nPurpose:\n\n> Temporary safe baseline for external/customer-facing AgentifyMe workers, including Speed-to-Lead workers.\n\nThis is explicitly a **temporary architecture**, not necessarily the final long-term Agent State solution.\n\n### Allowed reuse from `hermes-base-setup`\n\nSanitized generic mechanisms may be copied/adapted:\n\n```text\n.gitignore strategy\nsecret scanning\nsafe bootstrap mechanics\nversion/build metadata\nstate-sync patterns\ngeneric deployment helpers\ngeneric documentation structure\n```\n\n### Explicitly forbidden content\n\nDo **not** copy:\n\n```text\nStartup Teams internal profiles\nSOUL/persona files containing internal company context\ninternal memories\ninternal skills\ncompany-only instructions\ninternal customer information\nAPI keys/tokens/secrets\nemployee-specific context\nprivate operational notes\n```\n\n### One-time contamination validation\n\nDuring repository creation:\n\n1. create the sanitized baseline in a temporary worktree/directory;\n2. scan it for known internal profile/memory/skill markers;\n3. inspect Git history/content before first production use;\n4. fail the setup if internal Startup Teams content is found;\n5. correct the source tree before use.\n\nA permanent continuous “internal marker” test is **not required** at this time.\n\nNormal secret scanning remains required.\n\n### Speed-to-Lead\n\nSpeed-to-Lead worker agents may temporarily use this repository as their default agent baseline.\n\nDo not copy the internal `hermes-base-setup` agent branches into it.\n\n---\n\n# 2. Baseline from latest handoff\n\nDo not redo completed work.\n\nExpected production baseline:\n\n```text\nACMS CT122:\n  6f772aa\n  v0.11.0\n  alembic 0008\n\nLLM Manager / ARM VM114:\n  cb82bcd\n  ORM slices 1–4 live\n  Server Manager API healthy\n\nVM124 / acms-worker-001:\n  healthy\n  Bridge proven\n  real budget-gated dispatch proven\n  state sync VERIFIED\n\nAttention:\n  API + UI + SSE live\n\nRecovery:\n  ARM max 5 attempts\n  ACMS max 3 Executive reassignments\n```\n\nVerify current `main`, production SHA, health, and migrations before modifying anything.\n\nIf production moved forward compatibly, use current main rather than resetting to these SHAs.\n\n---\n\n# 3. Phase A — enforce AI-safe branch protection\n\nThis is now explicitly authorized.\n\n## A1. Inventory repositories AI agents modify\n\nAt minimum inspect:\n\n```text\nstartupteams/acms-project-framework\nstartupteams/llm-manager-project-framework\nstartupteams/pdu-marion-ia-usa-project-framework\nstartupteams/hermes-base-setup\nstartupteams/agentifyme-speed-to-lead\n```\n\nAlso include any other repository currently modified by autonomous AI agents.\n\nDo not blindly modify unrelated archival repositories.\n\n## A2. Protection policy\n\nFor each active production/default branch:\n\n```text\nrequire pull request before merge\nrequire existing CI/status checks\nAI/machine identity cannot push directly to protected branch\nAI/machine identity cannot bypass required checks\nno normal force push\nno normal branch deletion\n```\n\nDo **not** require a human approving review merely to merge routine AI-generated PRs unless that repository already has such a requirement.\n\nPreserve:\n\n```text\nhuman administrator emergency bypass/direct-main capability\n```\n\nDocument who/what is considered:\n\n```text\nhuman admin\nAI agent identity\ndeployment identity\nCI identity\n```\n\n## A3. Verification\n\nPerform a harmless verification:\n\n```text\nAI identity direct push → rejected\nPR with failing required check → cannot merge\nPR with all required checks → automated merge path works\nhuman admin emergency path remains documented/available\n```\n\nDo not intentionally break production to test this.\n\n## A4. Documentation\n\nCreate/update:\n\n```text\ndocs/operations/BRANCH_PROTECTION_AND_AI_MERGE_POLICY.md\n```\n\nCheckpoint.\n\n---\n\n# 4. Phase B — execute approved GPU retention architecture\n\nImplement Option A safely.\n\n## B1. Re-measure before migration\n\nRecord:\n\n```text\nrow count\ntable size\ndatabase size\noldest/newest sample\napprox rows/day\napprox MB/day\nimportant query patterns\n```\n\nDo not assume the last handoff's values are still exact.\n\n## B2. Create hourly rollup\n\nAdd an authoritative aggregate table, such as:\n\n```text\ngpu_samples_hourly\n```\n\nUse the actual existing schema and metrics rather than inventing columns.\n\nAggregation must preserve the operational statistics required by LLM Manager.\n\nTypical concepts may include where supported:\n\n```text\nhost/GPU identity\nhour bucket\nsample_count\nmin/max/avg utilization\nmemory metrics\ntemperature\npower\nother currently queried telemetry\n```\n\n## B3. Backfill\n\nBackfill all existing raw history.\n\nUse a resumable/idempotent strategy if practical.\n\n## B4. Verification\n\nFor representative windows compare raw calculations against rollup rows.\n\nRequire deterministic tolerance rules for numeric aggregation.\n\nNo pruning until verification passes.\n\n## B5. Retention configuration\n\nConfigure:\n\n```text\nraw retention = 90 days\nhourly rollup retention = 730 days / 2 years\n```\n\nValues should be configuration-backed rather than embedded in a one-off SQL script.\n\n## B6. Backup\n\nConfirm recoverable backup/recovery point before first destructive prune.\n\n## B7. Dry-run prune\n\nCalculate exactly:\n\n```text\ncutoff\ncandidate row count\ncandidate size\noldest row after prune\n```\n\nLog the dry-run result.\n\n## B8. Execute pruning\n\nAfter all gates above pass:\n\n```text\ndelete only raw rows older than configured retention\nretain hourly aggregates\n```\n\nUse bounded transactions/batches if table behavior requires it.\n\n## B9. Scheduled maintenance\n\nAdd a scheduled rollup + retention job with:\n\n```text\nlocking\nidempotency\nmetrics/logs\nfailure alert/Attention path\n```\n\n## B10. Partitioning\n\nDo not partition now.\n\nCreate/retain TDR/Future Work trigger such as:\n\n```text\npartition if:\ntable growth materially accelerates\nretention delete becomes operationally expensive\nquery latency crosses threshold\nmaintenance/vacuum becomes problematic\n```\n\nCheckpoint.\n\n---\n\n# 5. Phase C — accept and enforce ADR-0011 governance\n\n## C1. ADR\n\nChange ADR-0011:\n\n```text\nProposed → Accepted\n```\n\nInclude the human clarification from this plan.\n\n## C2. Canonical hierarchy\n\nDocument:\n\n```text\nhuman business goal / Jira issue / Feature\n    ↓\nExecutive Agent\n    ├─ Work Item A = measurable goal\n    │   ├─ task/session/worker\n    │   ├─ task/session/worker\n    │   └─ measurable deliverable\n    ├─ Work Item B = measurable goal\n    └─ Work Item C = measurable goal\n```\n\nA Work Item may use multiple agents.\n\n## C3. Creation authorization\n\nEnforce at API/service layer if the current roles support it cleanly:\n\n```text\nAdministrator → may create Work Items\nExecutive Agent → may create Work Items within delegated scope\nordinary Worker → may NOT create Work Items\nObserver → may NOT create Work Items\n```\n\nDo not rely only on UI hiding.\n\nIf “Executive Agent” is not currently a first-class authorization concept, implement the smallest explicit capability/delegation model rather than checking an agent's display name.\n\n## C4. Worker recommendation path\n\nWorkers need a safe way to say:\n\n```text\nI found additional work\n```\n\nwithout creating it.\n\nReuse handoff/Attention/proposal mechanisms where practical.\n\nRecommended structure:\n\n```text\nproposed_next_work:\n  summary\n  reason\n  dependency\n  suggested acceptance criteria\n```\n\nExecutive Agent decides whether to turn it into a Work Item.\n\n## C5. `/new` behavior\n\nDo not create new Work merely because:\n\n```text\ncontext rotated\nsession restarted\nmodel changed\nagent process restarted\n```\n\nif the measurable goal remains the same.\n\nCheckpoint.\n\n---\n\n# 6. Phase D — Jira ↔ Executive Agent v1 integration\n\nImplement the approved **Jira-human / ACMS-execution** model.\n\n## D1. Architecture\n\n```text\nJira issue\n  = human-facing work / goal\n\nExecutive Agent\n  = conceptual AI assignee/orchestrator\n\nACMS Work Items\n  = internal AI decomposition\n\nWorker tasks/sessions\n  = internal execution\n```\n\n## D2. Authentication\n\nUse the designated authorized Jira account/service identity.\n\nNever embed credentials in source.\n\nSupport environment/secret-managed configuration.\n\nIf a Jira API token/site URL/project mapping is not available to the agent:\n\n- implement/test the integration contract with mock/sandbox configuration;\n- create an exact human setup note;\n- do not fabricate credentials.\n\n## D3. Inbound synchronization\n\nImplement a bounded poll/webhook integration that can detect Jira work assigned to the Executive Agent/service identity.\n\nCapture:\n\n```text\nJira issue key\nsummary\ndescription\npriority\nlabels\nstatus\nhuman comments/context\nlinks/attachments metadata where safely supported\n```\n\nCreate/link the corresponding high-level ACMS work context.\n\nDo not create hundreds of ACMS Work Items automatically from arbitrary Jira text without Executive decomposition.\n\n## D4. Executive decomposition\n\nThe Executive Agent may:\n\n```text\nread assigned Jira issue\nunderstand human goal\ncreate one or more ACMS Work Items\ndelegate them internally\ntrack progress\n```\n\nSubordinate workers do not create Jira issues.\n\n## D5. Outbound Jira updates\n\nAutomatically post useful summaries rather than noisy event streams.\n\nExamples:\n\n```text\nexecution started\nmajor milestone completed\nblocked / Human Attention required\nPR/artifact available\ngoal completed\nfinal handoff summary\n```\n\nInclude links/correlation IDs where possible.\n\nDo not post every tool call/session event.\n\n## D6. Jira status mutation\n\nFor v1, keep Jira status changes conservative.\n\nComments/summaries/links are authorized.\n\nIf automatic Jira status transitions are necessary, implement behind an explicit config flag defaulting OFF unless an existing human workflow clearly defines the transitions.\n\n## D7. No Jira microtask explosion\n\nExplicit invariant:\n\n```text\nACMS Work Item creation\n≠\nJira ticket creation\n```\n\nDo not create separate Jira tickets for normal internal AI decomposition.\n\n## D8. Linear\n\nDo not implement a new Linear-native path now.\n\nDocument that existing Jira↔Linear synchronization remains sufficient fallback.\n\nCheckpoint.\n\n---\n\n# 7. Phase E — create private external-agent baseline repository\n\nCreation is now authorized.\n\n## E1. Create repo\n\nCreate:\n\n```text\nstartupteams/agentifyme-external-agent-base\n```\n\nVisibility:\n\n```text\nPRIVATE\n```\n\n## E2. Build from sanitized generic mechanisms\n\nUse `hermes-base-setup` only as a **source of generic patterns**.\n\nDo not fork/copy history blindly if that risks retaining internal content in Git history.\n\nPrefer constructing a clean repository history from reviewed safe files.\n\nAllowed categories:\n\n```text\n.gitignore\nsecret scanner / pre-commit safety\nbootstrap skeleton\nversion metadata\ngeneric state sync\ngeneric README\ngeneric service/timer templates\n```\n\n## E3. Explicit deny list\n\nBefore first commit, scan for:\n\n```text\nSOUL.md internal content\nUSER.md internal employee content\nMEMORY.md internal context\nprofiles/\nmemories/\nskills/\nStartup Teams internal operating instructions\ncustomer/internal names not intended for product baseline\nsecrets\nAPI keys\ntokens\nprivate key

{"content": "1|\"\"\"ORM Slice 5 — write-path parity for the hosts/model_registry/recovery domains.\n2|\n3|TDR-0008 rule: implement the SQLAlchemy repository/service for each legacy\n4|psycopg2 write path, prove parity (row-count + checksum), and only then switch\n5|the adapter. This module provides the WRITE repositories; the legacy v011_core\n6|writes remain live until each is verified and cutover is authorized.\n7|\n8|Write domains covered (from the 2026-09-29 inventory of v011_core.py):\n9|    - hosts:        update node/vmid by guest_ip; set desired_power_state;\n10|                    set management_mode\n11|    - model_registry: upsert deployment rows; mark unroutable (health/routable)\n12|    - recovery_events: insert events\n13|Parity contract: every method mirrors the legacy SQL semantics EXACTLY\n14|(same columns, same WHERE, same updated_at handling) so a row-for-row diff\n15|after a shadow run is the verification (run_parity_check()).\n16|\"\"\"\n17|from __future__ import annotations\n18|\n19|from datetime import datetime, timezone\n20|\n21|from sqlalchemy import text\n22|from sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio import AsyncSession\n23|\n24|from ..models.orm import HostRecord, ModelRegistryRecord\n25|\n26|\n27|class HostWrites:\n28|    \"\"\"hosts-table writes (legacy: v011_core UPDATE hosts ...).\"\"\"\n29|\n30|    @staticmethod\n31|    async def update_node_vmid_by_ip(db: AsyncSession, *, guest_ip: str,\n32|                                     node: str, vmid: int) -> int:\n33|        # legacy: UPDATE hosts SET node=%s, vmid=%s WHERE guest_ip=%s\n34|        result = await db.execute(\n35|            HostRecord.__table__.update()\n36|            .where(HostRecord.__table__.c.guest_ip == guest_ip)\n37|            .values(node=node, vmid=vmid)\n38|        )\n39|        await db.commit()\n40|        return result.rowcount or 0\n41|\n42|    @staticmethod\n43|    async def set_desired_power_state(db: AsyncSession, *, guest_ip: str,\n44|                                      desired_power_state: str) -> int:\n45|        # ### [2026-09-29T14:59:33.439292+00:00] tool · tool: read_file

{"content": "1|# TDR-0008 — llmmanager DB ORM migration is staged, not complete\n2|\n3|Status: Accepted (REV4 §6 allows staged/behavior-preserving migration)\n4|\n5|## Context\n6|\n7|The llmmanager database has 20+ public tables + an agentmanager schema, all created by raw\n8|SQL (db/schema/schema.sql) and accessed by psycopg2 in the 2,600-line single-file app.\n9|Migrating all of it to SQLAlchemy ORM in one sprint risks regressions in the revenue-critical\n10|LLM serving path.\n11|\n12|## Decision\n13|\n14|- This sprint migrates ONLY the new agent_runtime_manager database to ORM+Alembic (greenfield).\n15|- LLM Manager machine API reads (model-routes, usage) go through SQLAlchemy Core `text()`\n16|  queries against the EXISTING schema — no schema change, no ORM models for those tables yet.\n17|- Existing write paths stay on the proven psycopg2 app code.\n18|- Follow-up sprints migrate tables group by group (deployments/hosts/model_registry first,\n19|  telemetry second) behind the adapter layer, each with row-count + checksum verification per REV4 §6.\n20|\n21|## Consequences\n22|\n23|- Two persistence styles coexist temporarily in the llmmanager DB (raw SQL + Core reads).\n24|- `server_manager.llm_manager.adapters` isolates the eventual cutover.\n25|\n26|## Slice status log (2026-09-29)\n27|\n28|- **Slice 1 (merged #47):** hosts / active_hosts / model_registry reflective ORM.\n29|- **Slices 2–3 (merged #49):** deployment_revisions / deployments + benchmark_runs /\n30|  qualification_envelopes reflective ORM + 4 read endpoints.\n31|- **Slice 4 (this phase, read-only inventory + scoped models):**\n32|  - **gpu_samples retention dependency:** gpu_samples is 197 MB / 1,591,369 rows of a 225 MB DB\n33|    (re-measured 2026-09-29; prior proposal said 191 MB / 1.54M — growth ~ +5 MB/week at the same\n34|    order). Retention decision STILL PENDING (human). Any ORM work that would WRITE to gpu_samples\n35|    or change its schema stays blocked; the retention proposal remains\n36|    GPU_SAMPLES_RETENTION_PROPOSAL (rollup table + N-day raw window; nothing deleted without\n37|    approval).\n38|  - **NOT blocked by retention:** electricity_rates, emporia_devices, emporia_channel_map,\n39|    facility_power_samples, host_power_rollup_1m, facility_power_rollup_1d, recovery_events,\n40|    request_routing_log, manager_settings, rdma_ring_nodes, multi_node_deployments — read-only\n41|    ORM parity for these is safe and additive.\n42|- **Slices 5–6 (planned):** 5 = write-path parity behind the adapter layer (verify by row-count +\n43|  checksum before cutover); 6 = remaining telemetry tables AFTER the retention decision lands.\n44|  Legacy psycopg2 paths stay until write parity is proven (TDR-0008 rule).\n45|", "total_lines": 44, "file_size": 2576, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

{"status": "success", "output": "\"\"\"active_deployments — §21.7 DB-backed source of truth for active placement.\n\nReplaces hard-coded Python constants as the authority for \"which VM is the\nactive deployment on this physical host\". All consumers (dashboard/status API,\nhealth probes, recovery, VM power control, registry sync, deployment-control\nAPIs, hosting-command presets, routing) derive the active target from here.\n\nData model (per plan §21.7):\n\n    physical host      active_hosts      physical_host TEXT PRIMARY KEY\n    -> active deployment                 active_host_ip TEXT NOT NULL\n    -> active VM                         updated_at, updated_by\n\nUsage:\n    from active_deployments import active_ip_for, set_active, ACTIVE_HOSTS_TABLE\n\nThe `hosts` table remains the full inventory; `active_hosts` only records WHICH\ninventory row is active per physical host. Historical deployment revisions and\nRDMA ring data are never touched by this module (§21.4).\n\nFallback behavior: if the table/DB is unavailable, consumers fall back to the\nlegacy module-level maps so the system degrades to v011 behavior instead of\nbreaking (documented TDR).\n\"\"\"\nimport os\nimport threading\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\n\n_CACHE = {}\n_CACHE_TTL_S = 30.0\n_cache_lock = threading.Lock()\n\nDDL = \"\"\"\nCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS active_hosts (\n    physical_host TEXT PRIMARY KEY,\n    active_host_ip TEXT NOT NULL,\n    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now(),\n    updated_by TEXT\n)\"\"\"\n", "tool_calls_made": 1, "duration_seconds": 0.03}

VM103 / 10.0.20.161 remains mapped for admin power\n# actions on the rollback VM; desired-state gating (STOPPED_INTENTIONAL) is what\n# keeps recovery from auto-starting it (§21.8).\nNODE_MAP = {\n    \"10.0.20.168\": (\"miam00111\", 102),\n    \"10.0.20.161\": (\"miam00111\", 103),\n    \"10.0.20.162\": (\"miam00112\", 401),\n    \"10.0.20.163\": (\"miam00143\", 109),\n    \"10.0.20.164\": (\"miam00144\", 111),\n    \"10.0.20.165\": (\"miam-00149\", 149),\n}\nHOST_IPS = list(NODE_MAP.keys())\nPVE_HOST = \"10.0.20.135\"\n\nRECOVERY_WINDOW_MIN = 30      # lookback window for bounded attempts\nVM_START_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 2     # §B4 defaults\nSERVICE_RESTART_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 2\nSERVICE_RESTART_COOLDOWN_MIN = 15\n\nROUTER = APIRouter()\n\n\ndef sec(name):\n    try:\n        with open(os.path.join(SECRETS, name)) as f:\n            return f.read().strip()\n    except Exception:\n        return None\n\n\ndef _pg_pw():\n    try:\n        with open(PG_PW_FILE) as f:\n            for line in f:\n                if line.startswith(\"PG_PW=\"):\n                    return line.strip().split(\"=\", 1)[1]\n    except Exception:\n        pass\n    return None\n\n\ndef pg():\n", "tool_calls_made": 1, "duration_seconds": 0.03}
